# OT2004 Current-Term Neutral Projection

## Opening authority and chronology

The opening authority is the synchronized [Holdings volumes](../../../state/holdings/INDEX.md), [Standards and Tests](../../../state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md), and [Standing State](../../../state/STANDING_STATE.md), edition November 22, 2026. All three identify OT2003 as the last completed term and carry this common cutoff: June 30, 2004, after all 119 completed OT2003 Court events and the admitted Rule 53 amendment effective December 1, 2003. The [continuous Holdings view](../../../state/HOLDINGS.md) matches all 23 canonical volumes. These repository trackers govern any inconsistent descriptive reference in the supplied index.

105 scheduled OT2004 Court events in chunks 1–10 are complete through June 27, 2005. The last completed event in assignment order is Van Orden v. Perry. Gonzalez v. Crosby is decided June 23, 2005, and McCreary County v. American Civil Liberties Union of Kentucky is decided June 27, 2005. The retained original proceedings remain at their recorded stages. The close candidates are staged; completion of the full-term audit and coordinated publication remain pending. Assignment counts include only actual earlier Records, with same-date case names taken alphabetically; an earlier-dated Record written later reopens no completed assignment.

Each uncoordinated same-date event used the law effective before that date. The completed same-date groups combine prospectively; their events supply law only to later dates unless an express coordination order provides otherwise. Assignment counts separately include earlier-date Records and preceding same-date case names in alphabetical order; prior assignments remain as made.

The manifest and ledger project 105 completed scheduled Court events and one admitted noncase-law source, section 703 of the American Jobs Creation Act. The three unscheduled originals and the retained Kansas v. Colorado proceedings remain governed by their existing conditions; completing a scheduled event does not invent a later terminal disposition. Opening validation remains at [OPENING_VALIDATION.md](../audit/OPENING_VALIDATION.md).

## Current law

The opening trackers remain the baseline; the following completed Records add controlling law at their effective dates. Their Public Projections supply the complete operative rules, authority, explanations and remedies. These summaries preserve the material distinctions needed for continuation; the underlying Records control any conflict.

- **October 22 — [American Jobs Creation Act, section 703](../records/american-jobs-creation-act-section-703_source_2004-10-22.md).** Enacted noncase law adds then-section 62(a)(19) treatment for otherwise allowable attorney-fee and court-cost deductions on the covered discrimination, employment, civil-rights, retaliation, specified false-claim and Medicare secondary-payer actions defined in section 703. The deduction cannot exceed the amount included in gross income for that taxable year from the relevant judgment or settlement. Both the payment of fees or costs and the judgment or settlement must occur after October 22, 2004. The exact enacted coverage, exceptions and dual temporal trigger are preserved in the source Record. Banks and Banaitis concern earlier transactions and retain their applicable tax-year law.

- **November 9 — [Leocal v. Ashcroft](../records/leocal-v-ashcroft_merits_2004-11-09.md#public-projection).** O'Connor's unanimous opinion excludes an offense permitting accidental or negligent injury without employment of force against another from both parts of 18 U.S.C. §16. Elements and the applicable conviction record govern; injury cannot supply force use, and risk of injury cannot substitute for the specified risk of force use during the felony. A court may decide a disputed criminal predicate before applying the review restriction. The DUI aggravated-felony ground must be removed and continuing immigration consequences corrected through the reviewing court and agency; the convictions remain valid. A recklessness-specific offense is outside the presented question. Bailey and Taylor are applied; Calcano-Martinez is distinguished on its conceded predicates.

- **November 15 — [Smith v. Texas](../records/smith-v-texas_merits_2004-11-15.md#public-projection).** Souter's seven-Justice opinion applies Tennard's rejection of severe-permanent-impairment and crime-nexus relevance screens and Penry's requirement of a lawful, coherent life-verdict mechanism for independent culpability mitigation. A negative answer still certifying factual doubt the juror may not hold does not supply that mechanism. Relevance prescribes no favorable weight or disability diagnosis. The state judgment is reversed on both merits grounds. If the restriction was preserved and no remaining procedural issue precludes relief, the State must prove beyond reasonable doubt on the full penalty record that it did not contribute to death; failure requires adequate capital resentencing or a lawful noncapital sentence within a reasonable correction period. Guilt remains intact. Scalia and Thomas dissent; seven directly support every holding.

- **November 15 — [United States v. $100,348.00 in U.S. Currency](../records/united-states-v-100348-us-currency_merits_2004-11-15.md#public-projection).** Thomas's Parts I–II are unanimous: a lawful possessor may protect an established property interest or enforceable obligations throughout the property they cover, despite another person's title; this first-party protection does not revive the owner's defaulted claim. Partly punitive reporting forfeiture receives gross-disproportionality review, with the claimant's burden extended from Wagoner County, independent constitutional review and clear-error review of supported facts. Six Justices sustain $10,000 and reject $100,348; $90,348 must be returned to the legally entitled claimant. The whole-fund interest includes the established $100,000 bailment, without a personal-title finding for the remaining $348. The lower hypothetical fine level requires all four source/knowledge/recklessness/purpose conditions; the constitutional result holds under either $5,000 or $30,000 benchmark without inventing missing findings. The pre-CAFRA regime, separate civil forfeitability and independent fee/default rulings remain intact. O'Connor, Scalia and Kennedy dissent only on amount.

- **November 30 — [Anderson v. Spear](../records/anderson-v-spear_merits_2004-11-30.md#public-projection).** Kennedy's Part I has seven direct joins applying Burson's strict scrutiny to ordinary public streets and sidewalks; the unsupported 500-foot electioneering reach, including nonintimidating write-in instructions, fails. The exit-polling exclusion and confined indoor ownership exception do not restore the public-forum speech. All nine invalidate the $50,000 personal-loan ceiling and complete post-election contribution ban: initial personal funding and later donor repayment are distinct, and actual contributions remain subject to justified, closely fitted regulation. All nine support vacatur of the universal express-advocacy-only construction; eight join that ground, while Stone-Zsela joins that component's judgment alone. The decree must correct the policies' unlawful operation unless the State shows narrower relief fully cures the violation, preserve independently lawful rules and determine lawful severability. Scalia and Thomas have separate speech-method grounds.

- **November 30 — [Koons Buick Pontiac GMC, Inc. v. Nigh](../records/koons-buick-v-nigh_merits_2004-11-30.md#public-projection).** Ginsburg's eight-Justice opinion holds that the operative §1640(a)(2)(A) retains the $100-to-$1,000 individual-action range for clause (i), with the specific $200-to-$2,000 range for credit not under an open-end plan and secured by real property or a dwelling. The successive enacted structure gives effect to both ranges. The $24,192.80 statutory award is reversed for the $1,000 ceiling; independent actual damages, liability and fee entitlement retain their own bases. Thomas joins this actual text-and-structure explanation in full; Scalia dissents on amended grammar. Lamie and Hartford Underwriters are applied.

- **December 6 — [San Diego v. Roe](../records/san-diego-v-roe_merits_2004-12-06.md#public-projection).** Stevens's eight-Justice opinion restores dismissal of the reviewed employment-speech theory. The particular police-associated commercial performance fails Connick's content/form/context public-concern inquiry; public availability, compensation and lawful sexual content do not themselves establish that threshold. NTEU's protection of unrelated citizen expression and broad-prospective-ban rule remain intact; Pickering balancing is not triggered on this expression. The Court makes no operational-disruption finding as a premise. Stone-Zsela dissents on the protected-expression threshold and would require the ordinary individual employment inquiry.

- **December 6 — [Theofel v. Farey-Jones](../records/theofel-v-farey-jones_merits_2004-12-06.md#public-projection).** Stone-Zsela's unanimous opinion recognizes genuine provider backup storage under §2510(17)(B) after delivery or recipient access, requiring proof of the actual backup function by a preponderance. A sole archive without that function is insufficient. Under §2701(c)(1), physical disclosure is not conclusive authorization when consciously procured by materially deceptive invocation of patently invalid compulsory process going to the essential nature of access; negligence, routine irregularity and collateral deception do not meet that rule. Actual unauthorized access, defendant-specific mental state, remaining §2707 predicates and supported good-faith or authorization defenses remain for adjudication. Both categorical dismissal grounds are rejected; distinct statutory and state-law branches remain at their existing scope. Konop's interception and actual-user rules retain their separate operation.

- **December 7 — [Kansas v. Colorado](../records/kansas-v-colorado_exceptions_2004-12-07.md#public-projection).** Breyer's unanimous opinion resolves six exceptions and retains the original action. The Master must use the binding 1969 method for additional interest on qualifying losses incurred from 1969 onward, never before loss, preserving separately recoverable earlier principal and inflation and excluding duplicated inflation or speculative returns. The rolling ten-year H-I window is accepted as evidence under the supported error findings; it cannot authorize forbidden accumulated credits or extinguish independently proved usable-water depletion. Initial Colorado credit decisions require independent federal review of every issue essential to state-line compliance. The annual-period objection to 1997–1999 compliance is rejected on Kansas's own figures; distinct material technical objections remain returnable. The current River Master request is denied, and justified technical deferrals remain subject to a concrete, timely return through the existing reference. The Master must prepare a corrected calculation and conforming decree. Kansas owns any recovery.

- **December 8 — [KP Permanent Make-Up v. Lasting Impression](../records/kp-permanent-make-up-v-lasting-impression_merits_2004-12-08.md#public-projection).** Scalia's unanimous opinion keeps likely confusion with the infringement claimant, including an incontestable registration, and places the ordinary civil burden of §1115(b)(4)'s conditions on the defense claimant: use otherwise than as a mark, fairly and in good faith, only to describe its goods or services or geographic origin. Some likely confusion can coexist with fair use; relevant confusion evidence may illuminate the actual defense conditions without shifting infringement proof. The no-confusion burden is vacated; ordinary summary-judgment proceedings determine actual presentation and protect a complete fair use established without genuine dispute. Park 'N Fly, Qualitex and TrafFix retain their actual boundaries.

- **December 13 — [Brosseau v. Haugen](../records/brosseau-v-haugen_merits_2004-12-13.md#public-projection).** Kennedy's seven-Justice opinion grants personal qualified immunity and ends the reviewed individual federal damages claim. The plaintiff-favorable account includes a stationary Jeep, clear escape route and no identified immediate person-or-weapon threat, together with determined resistance, imminent departure and nearby occupied cars. Pre-February 21, 1999 law did not make this anticipatory-force boundary sufficiently clear; obvious applications may give notice without a factual twin, and there is no categorical vehicle/flight exception. Harlow and Anderson remain operative. Saucier supplies no mandatory merits-first rule; later Hope does not supply earlier notice. Force merits lie outside the limited grant, and separate municipal/state branches retain their status. Stevens and Stone-Zsela dissent separately.

- **December 13 — [Devenpeck v. Alford](../records/devenpeck-v-alford_merits_2004-12-13.md#public-projection).** O'Connor's substantive rule and remand grounds have all nine joins: actual contemporaneously known facts may establish probable cause for a different, unrelated offense; the announced label does not confine objective authority. The alternative must satisfy the actual offense elements and justify the restraint imposed. Whren and Pringle apply; Atwater and Ricci preserve independent requirements for added fine-only custody. Eight Associates reverse; Stone-Zsela would vacate the challenged ruling and joins the complete substantive rule and remand, withholding only formal reversal. Actual impersonation and obstruction applications, any properly presented custody issue and personal immunity remain for adjudication. Conduct-date immunity uses November 22, 1997; same-day Brosseau supplies no entering law.

- **2004-12-13 — [Educational Credit Management Corp. v. Polleys](../records/educational-credit-management-v-polleys_merits_2004-12-13.md#public-projection).** Tenth Circuit affirmed, 9–0; the adjudicated student-loan debt remains discharged. A debtor establishes undue hardship under the operative 11 U.S.C. §523(a)(8) by proving, more likely than not, that repayment would prevent a minimal standard of living for the debtor and dependents, that specific circumstances make that inability likely to persist for a significant portion of the repayment period, and that the claimed hardship accords with reasonable good-faith efforts to earn income, keep expenses reasonable and meet the obligation when able. Courts may not demand certainty of hopelessness, permanent disability or a payment the debtor lacked resources to make; mere inconvenience, deliberately contrived inability and unsupported pessimism do not establish the exception. The affirmed discharge continues to bar collection of the covered obligation under the applicable discharge injunction. ECMC must treat that debt as discharged; ordinary bankruptcy enforcement addresses violation of the injunction.

- **December 13 — [Florida v. Nixon](../records/florida-v-nixon_merits_2004-12-13.md#public-projection).** Breyer's unanimous opinion rejects automatic deficiency and presumed prejudice from the absence of express consent where counsel adequately discusses a capital concession, the defendant neither agrees nor objects, and meaningful adversarial representation and an actual jury trial continue. Strickland requires actual reasonable-performance and reasonable-probability-prejudice adjudication under trial-era standards; Bell distinguishes specific choices from complete abandonment, and Williams supplies ordinary lawful-outcome prejudice. The automatic new-trial judgment is reversed for the preserved ordinary claims, including necessary preparation or consultation questions. A concession over an expressed contrary objective lies outside this record.

- **December 13 — [Kowalski v. Tesmer](../records/kowalski-v-tesmer_merits_2004-12-13.md#public-projection).** Stone-Zsela's six-Justice opinion rejects the attorneys' third-party claim for want of a concrete existing or specifically prevented client relationship. Their pleaded reduction in regular paid rotation appointments supports a personal economic theory, which the majority assumes without deciding Article III; it does not itself supply entitlement to assert unidentified clients' rights. Practical indigent-litigation barriers do not alone cure the relationship defect. The counsel merits remain open. The attorney claim must be dismissed and dependent relief removed, after identifying any independently surviving basis before altering other relief; the individuals' separate abstention disposition remains intact. Ginsburg dissents with Stevens and Souter on the contextual prospective relationship and particular access obstacle.

- **2005-01-11 — [Timken Co. v. United States](../records/timken-v-united-states_merits_2005-01-11.md#public-projection).** Federal Circuit affirmed on the granted zeroing issue, 9–0; the reviewed determination remains effective. Section 3512(c) does not bar a challenge brought under the domestic antidumping statute merely because the challenger invokes international obligations as an aid to interpreting that statute. Koyo's statutory challenge is therefore properly considered on its merits. Under the operative 19 U.S.C. §§1675(a), 1677(35) and 1677b(a), Commerce may, in an authorized administrative review, treat transactions whose export price or constructed export price exceeds normal value as zero when aggregating positive dumping margins, provided it observes the prescribed comparison adjustments and gives a reasoned explanation. Zeroing is permitted rather than compelled; a challenger must establish a statutory prohibition or a defect under the governing review standard, and neither the fair-comparison argument nor the cited international report establishes the requested offset requirement here. Commerce and the trade court apply the operative determination without an offset compelled by this challenge. Ordinary administration implements the affirmed judgment.

- **2005-01-11 — [Whitfield v. United States and Hall v. United States](../records/whitfield-v-united-states_merits_2005-01-11.md#public-projection).** Both Eleventh Circuit judgments affirmed on the overt-act instruction question, 9–0 in each docket. Section 1956(h) independently defines conspiracy to commit an offense specified in §1956 or §1957 and requires neither allegation nor proof of an additional overt act. The Government must prove the actual unlawful agreement, the defendant's knowing participation and the required unlawful object beyond a reasonable doubt; association alone is insufficient, and the requested overt-act instruction was properly refused in both cases. Both reviewed judgments remain in force against this instruction challenge.

- **2005-01-12 — [Clark v. Martinez](../records/clark-v-martinez_merits_2005-01-12.md#public-projection).** Martinez affirmed; Benitez reversed; both remanded for enforceable supervised-release protection, each 8–1. Section 1231(a)(6) permits detention of every category it covers, including inadmissible persons who have never been admitted, only for the period reasonably necessary to accomplish removal. When removal lacks a significant likelihood within the reasonably foreseeable future and no independent lawful custody ground applies, the person is entitled to enforceable release under lawful supervision; release does not grant admission. Six months, counted within the removal process rather than added after the ordinary ninety-day removal period, is a presumptively reasonable detention interval. After it, the detainee must give good reason to believe that removal lacks a significant likelihood within the reasonably foreseeable future, and the Government must sufficiently rebut that showing; the interval alone does not require release, and supported evidence that a longer period is reasonably necessary, including relevant evidence about an inadmissible class, may justify continued detention. Both matters return for implementation of enforceable supervised-release protection. The courts must give effect to the unrebutted removal-prospect findings and retain the lawful supervision authorized by the statute. A later custody action must have an actual new lawful basis and available review; repeated short parole periods cannot defeat the adjudicated duration limit.

- **2005-01-12 — [Jama v. Immigration and Customs Enforcement](../records/jama-v-immigration-and-customs-enforcement_merits_2005-01-12.md#public-projection).** Eighth Circuit affirmed on the claimed advance-acceptance requirement, 5–4. Section 1231(b)(2)(E)(iv) permits selection of the country of a person's birth without an additional advance-acceptance condition when the preceding statutory steps and that clause's own predicates are satisfied. Clause (vii)'s express acceptance requirement governs its residual destination, not clause (iv); the agency remains bound by independently applicable removal protections and custody limits. The Eighth Circuit judgment remains effective, and the acceptance-based bar is removed. Implementation must satisfy the actual destination sequence and any operative protection or stay. Independently presented protection and detention claims proceed through their applicable channels and on their own records.

- **2005-01-12 — [Nordyke v. King](../records/nordyke-v-king_merits_2005-01-12.md#public-projection).** Affirmed as to the facial First Amendment denial; vacated as to the Second Amendment denial and remanded; both components unanimous. Individuals may invoke the Second Amendment, as incorporated through the Fourteenth Amendment, against a county restriction burdening protected possession or acquisition necessary to protected keeping. Public ownership does not extinguish the right; the lower court must assess the claimants' actual injuries, covered conduct, practical burdens, required justification and remaining preliminary-relief requirements under the governing law. The claimant must first identify protected keeping, bearing, acquisition, necessary ammunition or transportation through text, history and precedent; the county must then justify the regulation at the level required by its actual burden. A severe core burden requires a compelling interest, narrow tailoring and no comparably effective materially less restrictive alternative; a substantial burden requires an important objective, a substantial relationship and no unnecessary or effective elimination of protected activity; an incidental, nonobstructive regulation receives reasonableness review while remaining subject to prohibitions against arbitrariness, discrimination and cumulative destruction of the right. Ordinary firearm possession and exchange do not become protected speech in every application because a show also includes advocacy, and isolated expressive displays do not establish a basis to invalidate the entire possession ordinance. A demonstrably communicative display receives the protection appropriate to its actual context; the claimant must establish that expressive application and the requested breadth of relief, and the county must satisfy the scrutiny applicable to a proved burden, with viewpoint discrimination separately prohibited. The case returns for prompt consideration of the actual arms burden, the county's required justification and all remaining preliminary-relief requirements, including the hardship balance the lower court did not decide. The facial speech denial stands. Any properly preserved concrete expressive-display claim requires its own factual inquiry and the applicable expression standard.

- **2005-01-12 — [United States v. Booker](../records/united-states-v-booker_merits_2005-01-12.md#public-projection).** Constitutional rule reaffirmed 6–3; binding Guidelines retained 5–4. Booker sentence vacatur affirmed and remanded, with all nine accepting vacatur once the constitutional premise governs and five supporting the mandatory-framework directions; Fanfan sentence affirmed on the presented challenge, 5–4. A fact legally necessary to increase a binding sentencing ceiling or floor must receive applicable grand-jury charging protection and be proved to a jury beyond a reasonable doubt, validly admitted as an element, or determined beyond a reasonable doubt after a lawful jury waiver. This rule governs present-offense and necessary recidivist facts, including those that increase a binding Federal Sentencing Guidelines range; judicial selection within punishment independently authorized by the verdict or valid admissions remains permissible. The Federal Sentencing Guidelines remain binding to the extent their ranges rest on constitutionally established facts; an unsupported enhancement must be withheld rather than used to replace Congress's mandatory system with an advisory one. Section 3553(b)(1), §3742(e) and the enacted review and remand provisions remain effective in their lawful applications, with judicial discretion exercised within the independently authorized range and lawful statutory departures. Booker's appellate sentence vacatur is affirmed, and his case is remanded for a replacement sentence within a lawfully established binding range, subject to correction of independently established calculation errors. Fanfan's refusal to enhance and resulting 78-month sentence are affirmed against the asserted entitlement to use the excluded findings; any independently preserved calculation issue remains for ordinary disposition. Booker returns for lawful resentencing under the retained binding framework. Fanfan's 78-month sentence stands against the enhancement challenge; any independently preserved calculation question proceeds ordinarily. The Government must identify an independent lawful procedural source before seeking additional enhancement factfinding. Ordinary appellate review continues under the surviving enacted provisions.

- **2005-01-24 — [Bell v. Cone, No. 04-394](../records/bell-v-cone_merits_2005-01-24.md#public-projection).** Vacate the Sixth Circuit's aggravator-based sentence-relief judgment and remand, 7–2; actual judicial narrowing conclusively settled by eight Associates; six support the complete confined mandate. A state court's failure to cite its settled narrowing precedent does not itself establish that it applied an unconstitutionally vague capital aggravator. Federal review must examine the authoritative construction and the state court's actual reasoning and result under the applicable review law, without inventing an adjudication or corrective step the record does not establish. Tennessee's 1984 opinion actually applied its authoritative narrowed heinousness category to Cone's killings, and the asserted failure of judicial narrowing is conclusively rejected. Under 28 U.S.C. §2254(b)(2), a court may deny a habeas claim on a sufficient merits ground despite nonexhaustion; that permission does not require denial, establish a state merits adjudication or dispose of a distinct constitutional theory. The Sixth Circuit's aggravator-based sentence-relief judgment is vacated, and the case is remanded to dispose of Cone's existing, distinct jury-guidance and weighing claim under its actual state adjudication, the applicable federal-law cutoff, §2254 and any preserved valid default. Judicial narrowing is settled; if no distinct available theory survives, relief on this ground must be denied, while an available constitutional sentencing error requires an authorized actual correction or the governing State-borne harmlessness determination. The Sixth Circuit must resolve the existing distinct jury-guidance and weighing theory under the mandate in Part III, denying relief if no available ground survives and applying the governing correction or effect requirements if a reviewable violation is established. Actual judicial narrowing may not be reconsidered. Other properly pending sentence claims continue under the 2002 mandate, and the convictions remain intact.

- **2005-01-24 — [Commissioner of Internal Revenue v. Banks, No. 03-892](../records/commissioner-v-banks_merits_2005-01-24.md#public-projection).** reverse both fee-exclusion judgments and remand, 9–0 in each docket. When a client retains ownership and control of an income-producing legal claim and counsel acts as the client's agent, the client's gross income under 26 U.S.C. §61 includes the contractual contingent fee paid from an otherwise taxable recovery, whether the payment passes through the client or goes directly to counsel and whether a state-law lien secures it. A taxpayer seeking exclusion must identify an applicable statutory exclusion or a genuinely different ownership arrangement; uncertainty about the recovery's value and the lawyer's labor do not alone transfer the claim. Both fee-exclusion judgments are reversed, and each docket returns for the lawful tax calculation using the full otherwise taxable recovery and deductions available under the applicable tax-year provisions. Section 703(c) of the American Jobs Creation Act of 2004 makes both payment of the fees and costs after October 22, 2004, and a judgment or settlement after that date necessary to the new deduction; these earlier transactions remain governed by their tax-year law. Both tax cases return for further proceedings consistent with the inclusion rule. Each court must calculate the affected liability from the actual payment and return records under the applicable tax-year law.

- **2005-01-24 — [Howell v. Mississippi, No. 03-9560](../records/howell-v-mississippi_dig_2005-01-24.md#public-projection).** writ dismissed as improvidently granted; Mississippi judgment remains operative. Howell's state-law instruction argument and its indirect chain of citations did not meaningfully identify his federal constitutional claim, and the Mississippi Supreme Court did not decide that claim. The writ is dismissed as improvidently granted on that actual-record ground. Proceedings on this writ are concluded. The Mississippi judgment remains operative.

- **2005-01-24 — [Illinois v. Caballes, No. 03-923](../records/illinois-v-caballes_merits_2005-01-24.md#public-projection).** vacate the Illinois Supreme Court's judgment and remand, 7–2. An exterior examination by a properly trained narcotics dog that is actually confined to detecting contraband requires no separate drug suspicion when officers have lawful access and the examination adds no unlawful restraint or other protected intrusion. The State must establish those predicates and ordinary probable cause for any later physical search; the dog's label and the later discovery of drugs do not substitute for them. The case returns to the Illinois courts to apply the corrected federal rule to the accepted lawful-stop premises and resolve genuinely preserved independent grounds. If no independent defect remains, the absence of separate drug suspicion supplies no basis for suppression.

- **2005-01-24 — [GDF Realty Investments, Ltd. v. Norton; Fifth Circuit No. 01-51099; 326 F.3d 622, rehearing denied, 362 F.3d 286](../records/gdf-realty-v-norton_merits_2005-01-24.md#public-projection).** The existing necessary-inclusion rule governs unauthorized taking of other intrastate listed species within the same valid interstate wildlife system. A local range or lack of a species-specific market does not require a constitutional exemption or an independent species-effects hearing. Courts must identify the actual class, valid interstate regulation and genuine population-protection or enforcement relationship, preserving challenges to an outside-class application, an invalid underlying regime or a nominal or pretextual relationship. The owners' constitutional challenge is concluded; statutory enforcement, permitting and any properly presented compensation claim retain their distinct legal requirements.

- **2005-01-24 — [Women’s Medical Professional Corp. v. Taft; Sixth Circuit No. 01-4124; 353 F.3d 436](../records/womens-medical-v-taft_merits_2005-01-24.md#public-projection).** The revised statute's complete sequence and exclusions govern coverage, and the categorical ordinary-D&E objection is resolved against the providers. Seven Justices require usable necessary-care protection for medically supported material risk of death or serious health harm from forced substitution, including the combination of grave mental danger from continued pregnancy and a method necessary for safe treatment. Comparative advantage alone does not establish necessity; a preexisting physical complication cannot be an exclusive constitutional prerequisite. The actual statutory conjunction and a construction permitted by Ohio law must be assessed against the supported clinical applications. Six Justices directly require the complete previability framework: challengers establish duty, stable affected population, causation and actual interference; baseline injury, obstructive purpose and substantial obstacles independently fail; remaining severe burdens require compelling justification and narrow tailoring; remaining meaningful burdens require substantiated justification, fit and answers to concrete adequate less burdensome alternatives. Clinical evidence and usable exceptions receive their actual weight. Those are governing rules; clinical application, permissible construction, severability and fitted relief are the work on remand. A proved common-policy violation receives protection for the governed patients and providers unless the State establishes a fully effective narrower remedy. Prompt interim relief and expedited treatment of genuinely unresolved facts implement the rule. The unappealed fault construction and independent civil-standing disposition remain effective.

- **2005-02-22 — [Karaha Bodas Co., L.L.C. v. Perusahaan Pertambangan Minyak Dan Gas Bumi Negara; review of 364 F.3d 274; no Supreme Court docket supplied](../records/karaha-bodas-v-pertamina_merits_2005-02-22.md#public-projection).** In Convention recognition proceedings, courts identify the national arbitral procedural regime before deciding whether an annulment satisfies Article V(1)(e). A substantive-law clause or particular contractual incorporation has its actual agreed effect; it does not alone give that country the Convention's supervisory role. A genuine selection of another arbitral procedural regime must be established from the agreement and applicable law. Article VI permits adjournment when its qualified-authority predicate is met and the enforcing court considers adjournment proper; suitable security additionally requires an application by the party seeking enforcement. The Indonesian order fails the qualifying-country condition here, leaving the existing recognition judgment operative.

- **2005-02-22 — [Smith v. Massachusetts; No. 03-8661](../records/smith-v-massachusetts_merits_2005-02-22.md#public-projection).** Completed substantive acquittals receive the same protection in a continuing jury trial as in other trial forms. Courts examine the actual ruling and procedural course; general interlocutory reconsideration authority cannot retrospectively reopen an accomplished insufficiency determination after the defense has proceeded. A genuinely tentative ruling remains subject to completion, while a completed acquittal forecloses renewed adjudication without a separate showing of tactical injury.

- **2005-02-22 — [Stewart v. Dutra Construction Co.; No. 03-814](../records/stewart-v-dutra_merits_2005-02-22.md#public-projection).** Courts determine statutory vessel status from practical water-transport use or capability and the craft's actual service condition. Industrial purpose, lack of self-propulsion and temporary immobility do not exclude an operational craft that carries people or equipment over water. Permanently fixed or withdrawn structures with only theoretical transportation capacity remain outside the definition. Worker status, actionable defendant capacity and negligence retain their distinct requirements. A correction of one claim's vessel premise carries the remedy warranted for that claim; independently adjudicated grounds retain their procedural force.

### 2005-02-23 — [Dowhal v. SmithKline Beecham Consumer Healthcare; California Supreme Court No. S109306; 32 Cal. 4th 910](../records/dowhal-v-smithkline-beecham_merits_2005-02-23.md#public-projection)

Section 379r(d)(2) exempts a state requirement adopted by a public initiative or referendum enacted before September 1, 1997 from that section's express displacement of nonidentical requirements. A saved requirement nevertheless yields when the party asserting preemption establishes incompatibility with a valid, specifically applicable federal command or supported regulatory method under other operative federal law.

**Authority:** Part I, joined by all nine Justices, controls this construction.

Under §§321(n), 352(a) and 355, the FDA may reject drug labeling that is literally accurate but materially misleading in context, considering its representations and material omissions concerning consequences under prescribed or customary use. That authority requires an authorized labeling determination supported by its actual reasons; a bare preference for how consumers should behave is insufficient.

**Authority:** Part I, joined by all nine Justices, controls this rule and the identification of the definitive federal action.

The reproductive-harm warning demanded for the covered nicotine-replacement products conflicts with the operative FDA decision because it restores the misleading comparative-risk message that decision rejected. The claim and its dependent remedies therefore cannot be enforced while that decision governs; the party asserting displacement must establish the relevant action's coverage and conflict for a materially different warning or duty.

**Authority:** Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Breyer, supplies a five-Justice controlling application.

**Affirmed, 5–4. Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Breyer. **Opposing:** Stevens, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg.

Judgment for defendants remains effective insofar as the requested enforcement and dependent monetary or injunctive relief require the warning that conflicts with the operative federal decision. Any genuinely different compatible duty that was pleaded and preserved retains its own adjudicative basis. The judgment concerns the identified message and covered products.

- Geier v. American Honda Motor Co. is applied: saving language and actual conflict remain separate inquiries, and displacement requires the actual federal choice and incompatible state obligation.
- Freightliner Corp. v. Myrick is applied: this case contains an operative federal determination, unlike an inference based on regulatory absence.
- Medtronic, Inc. v. Lohr is applied: approval alone supplies no general immunity, and genuinely compatible duties retain their force.
- FDA v. Brown & Williamson Tobacco Corp. supplies independent examination of statutory delegation; preemption does not enlarge the agency's authority.

The case returns for implementation of the affirmed preemption judgment at its stated scope. The state court must identify the covered products and warning demand when giving effect to that judgment and adjudicate any independently preserved compatible duty on its own basis.

### 2005-02-23 — [Johnson v. California; No. 03-636](../records/johnson-v-california_merits_2005-02-23.md#public-projection)

An express racial prison-housing classification must satisfy the common substantial-justification standard: the State must establish an important lawful objective, a substantial evidence-supported relationship between the classification and that objective, justified coverage and actual burdens, and a supported response to concrete practicable alternatives that offer comparable effectiveness with materially less racial disadvantage. Custody makes credible security experience, urgency and genuinely incomplete information relevant within that inquiry, while a protective purpose, general racial assumptions or administrative convenience alone cannot satisfy it.

**Authority:** Part I, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, controls.

A judgment sustaining the reception policy under materially more deferential review must be vacated for application of the common standard to the actual policy and record. Additional factfinding serves genuine material disputes about that application; the court must resolve issues the existing record already permits it to decide.

**Authority:** Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies the six-Justice judgment.

Individual damages depend on actionable conduct, causal responsibility and the qualified-immunity law governing when each defendant acted; prospective correction depends on continuing exposure and a responsible current official. Under 18 U.S.C. §3626(a)(1), a prison decree must be narrowly drawn, extend no further than necessary to correct the federal-right violation, use the least intrusive means necessary, and give substantial weight to adverse effects on public safety or operation of the criminal justice system; relief requiring or permitting an official to exceed state or local authority, or otherwise violate state or local law, is permissible only if federal law requires it to correct the violation, it is necessary, and no other relief will correct the violation.

**Authority:** Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, controls these independent requirements.

**Vacated and remanded, 6–3. Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer. **Opposing:** Stevens, who would hold the categorical policy unconstitutional now and remand for remaining relief, and Scalia and Thomas, who would affirm under Turner.

The lower court must evaluate the policy's coverage, duration, evidentiary support, burdens and concrete practicable alternatives under the government's burden. Further evidence is required only for genuinely unresolved material issues. Damages require separate determinations of individual conduct, causation and conduct-date immunity. Prospective relief requires current exposure, proper responsible officials and the statutory remedy findings. If the policy fails, relief must correct its operation for the persons it governs unless California establishes that a narrower remedy fully cures the violation, consistently with §3626.

- Adarand Constructors, Inc. v. Peña is applied to express prison-housing classifications: its common substantial-justification burden governs actual racial treatment.
- Brown v. City of Oneonta is applied: benign purpose does not remove deliberate racial selection from that burden.
- Turner v. Safley is distinguished as the source of general prison-regulation review; its deference does not replace the governing racial-classification rule.
- Overton v. Bazzetta retains force for the prison matters it decides and for the practical relevance of institutional evidence.
- Harlow v. Fitzgerald and Anderson v. Creighton supply the individual conduct-date immunity inquiry.
- Saucier v. Katz and Brosseau v. Haugen preserve separate adjudication of the constitutional merits and the existing personal defense, including obvious applications of existing law.

The district court must set a prompt schedule for resolving the relevant evidence and any supported interim-relief request under the applicable statutory conditions. It must identify current responsible officials and continuing exposure, decide any personal damages defenses and causal responsibility separately, and require a compliant housing practice if the common policy fails. Any injunction must specify permitted security exceptions and compliance dates consistently with §3626, so that it corrects the established violation through a workable decree.

### 2005-03-01 — [Cherokee Nation of Oklahoma v. Leavitt; Nos. 02-1472 and 03-853](../records/cherokee-nation-v-leavitt_merits_2005-03-01.md#public-projection)

When an authorized self-determination agreement promises reasonable, allowable contract support costs and the relevant appropriation legally provides sufficient funds for that undertaking, the agency's allocation to competing demands does not discharge the promise. An enacted funding limit or an actual contractual condition controls, but a committee-report expectation or internal budget allocation does not create such a limit.

Section 450j-1(b)'s separate provision that the Secretary is not required to reduce funding for programs, projects or activities serving one tribe to make funds available to another tribe or tribal organization permits the Secretary to decline that reduction. It neither prohibits every reduction nor defeats liability on these authorized contracts when other legally unrestricted funds suffice.

The annual provision making $7.5 million remain available until expended for the Indian Self-Determination Fund's transitional costs of initial or expanded tribal contracts establishes a designated fund with continuing availability. It does not make that fund the exclusive source for the support-cost promises at issue or cap liability at the agency's initial or expanded-program allocation when other legally available appropriations are sufficient.

Section 314 of Pub. L. 105-277 makes the amounts appropriated or earmarked in committee reports the total amounts available for the identified support costs in fiscal years 1994–1998. In these disputes, it limits use of remaining appropriations without retroactively extinguishing claims accrued under the earlier contractual undertakings; the agreements' congressional-adjustment clauses operate at that statutory scope.

| Judgment component | Disposition and vote | Supporting Justices | Remedy |
|---|---|---|---|
| No. 02-1472: Cherokee FY1997 and Shoshone-Paiute FY1996–1997 claims | Reversed, 9–0 | Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, Breyer | Return for contract adjudication under the resolved statutory constructions. |
| No. 03-853: Cherokee FY1994–1996 claims | Affirmed, 9–0 | Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, Breyer | The Board determination sustained by the Federal Circuit remains effective; return for implementation and any genuinely remaining proceedings. |

- Hercules, Inc. v. United States supplies the actual authorized undertaking requirement; the express support-cost promises satisfy that requirement rather than resting on general fairness.
- United States v. Winstar Corp. supplies enforcement of the actual financial undertaking and its real conditions while preserving Congress's legislative authority; its rule applies to the promises and funding qualifications here.
- Lincoln v. Vigil retains its rule for discretionary allocation of unrestricted lump sums. The additional statutory and contractual duties here distinguish that allocation question from liability for a binding promise.
- Lynch v. United States and Perry v. United States retain their protection of genuine government obligations and support the construction of section 314 that avoids attributing repudiation of accrued contractual claims to an appropriation-account limit.

The mandates return the two proceedings through their respective appellate courts. In No. 02-1472, the responsible tribunals adjudicate the contract claims under the Court's constructions, identifying and resolving any remaining annual amount, allowable-cost or loss issue, including any duplication of transferred program or administrative funding, through ordinary contract procedure. In No. 03-853, the sustained Board determination remains effective and proceeds to lawful implementation, with only genuinely remaining matters receiving further adjudication. Resulting awards use the statutory payment channel; agency underallocation cannot again substitute for a contractual defense.

### 2005-03-01 — [Petro-Hunt, L.L.C. v. United States; No. 04-190; judgment below, 365 F.3d 385](../records/petro-hunt-v-united-states_merits_2005-03-01.md#public-projection)

Federal common law governs the preclusive effect of the earlier federal-question judgment. A final title judgment binds the claim it adjudicated, including grounds that could have been advanced within that claim, but distinct servitudes with different acquisition and use histories do not become the same claim merely because they share owners, related conveyances or a legal premise; the party invoking preclusion must establish the required identity from the actual instruments and adjudication.

Mutual issue preclusion requires the identical issue to have been actually litigated and necessarily determined. Nebo Oil's adjudication of state-law retroactivity and constitutional objections, while assuming Louisiana law supplied the rule of decision, does not preclude the distinct federal choice-of-law issue now asserted.

**Affirmed and remanded, 9–0.**

**Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer.

Nebo Oil's adjudication of its original servitude remains effective. The Fifth Circuit's determination that the other 95 servitudes are subject to Louisiana's ten-year nonuse prescription remains effective; the district court must determine actual prescription and resulting title for each servitude.

- Nevada v. United States supplies the inquiry into the property right actually adjudicated and protects final title within that scope.
- Semtek International Inc. v. Lockheed Martin Corp. supplies the distinction between a judgment's source and actual external effect; its diversity-specific state-law incorporation rule remains confined to that setting.
- Plaut v. Spendthrift Farm, Inc. retains its protection of completed federal adjudications; applying preclusion to the decree's actual subject matter preserves that protection.
- Commissioner v. Sunnen and Montana v. United States supply the issue-identity and actual-adjudication requirements applied here.
- United States v. Stauffer Chemical Co. retains mutual issue preclusion where its predicates exist; the missing actual litigation distinguishes this case.

The district court must identify each disputed servitude's instrument, acquisition history, qualifying good-faith operations and interruptions, apply the governing prescription rule, and determine title. It must consider the asserted continuing operations on five servitudes, the Government's concessions concerning operations on some servitudes, and the second summary-judgment motion previously dismissed as moot. Any properly supported specific preclusion claim must satisfy the claim-identity or actually-litigated-issue requirements stated here. The fee cross-appeal lacks its former prevailing-party predicate after the blanket preclusion judgment falls; any later fee issue follows the eventual proper judgment.

### 2005-03-01 — [Roper v. Simmons; No. 03-633](../records/roper-v-simmons_merits_2005-03-01.md#public-projection)

The Eighth and Fourteenth Amendments prohibit the death penalty for an offense committed before the offender's eighteenth birthday. Age at the offense controls; later adulthood, the crime's severity or planning, and an assessment of exceptional individual maturity do not create an exception.

**Authority:** Kennedy's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, controls every holding and its explanation.

Stanford v. Kentucky is overruled insofar as it permits execution for offenses committed at sixteen or seventeen. Because the new rule removes a class from death eligibility, a death sentence within that class must be replaced through lawful noncapital sentencing procedures in nonfinal cases and final cases reached through an available review channel, preserving valid convictions and independent procedural requirements.

**Authority:** The same six-Justice opinion controls both the express precedent change and the application to Simmons's state collateral judgment.

**Affirmed, 6–3. Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer. **Opposing:** O'Connor, Scalia and Thomas.

Simmons's conviction and sentence of life imprisonment without eligibility for probation, parole or release except by gubernatorial action remain effective. Missouri must maintain that noncapital custody basis and remove authority to execute him for this offense. His established offense-date age supplies the categorical ground for relief.

- Stanford v. Kentucky is overruled to the extent it permits death for offenses committed before eighteen; that permission no longer has force.
- Atkins v. Virginia is extended in its categorical proportionality method, including domestic evidence, culpability, penal purposes and unreliable selection. Its separate disability definition and procedures retain their own scope.
- Thompson v. Oklahoma remains consistent with the result; its under-sixteen protection is encompassed by the present under-eighteen rule.

The affirmed Missouri judgment governs Simmons's custody and bars execution for this offense. His noncapital sentence remains subject to its stated gubernatorial-action qualification. Other qualifying death judgments receive the substantive age rule through their existing lawful review channels and the noncapital correction authorized by governing law.

### 2005-03-02 — [Catholic Charities of Sacramento, Inc. v. Superior Court; California Supreme Court No. S099822; 32 Cal. 4th 527](../records/catholic-charities-v-superior-court_jurisdiction_2005-03-02.md#public-projection)

A highest state court's comprehensive rejection of federal theories in reviewing denial of preliminary protection does not, by itself, establish a final judgment under §1257. Review requires a judgment concluding the relevant action or an established Cox exception, including practical completion with the further outcome preordained; the operative effect of the judgment governs rather than its interlocutory label.

**Authority:** Thomas's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, controls both holdings and every stated exception analysis.

Once the federal issue has been finally decided, the remaining Cox routes permit review when it necessarily survives and requires decision regardless of further proceedings; when effective later review cannot be obtained whatever their outcome; or when federal reversal would end the relevant litigation and delay seriously threatens federal policy. The invoking party must establish the actual predicates of a route, and useful later protection—not the abstract possibility of another appeal—governs the effective-review inquiry.

**Authority:** The same eight-Justice opinion controls the distinct analyses of the second, third and fourth categories.

**Dismissed for want of jurisdiction, 8–1. Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. **Opposing:** Stevens.

The dismissal ends this attempted review and leaves the California preliminary-relief disposition in its existing posture. Because §1257 jurisdiction is unestablished, the Court does not reach the free-exercise, establishment, exemption or related constitutional merits.

- Cox Broadcasting Corp. v. Cohn supplies four distinct practical-finality routes, each requiring its actual predicates.
- Richards v. Jefferson County is distinguished on operative effect: its conclusive claim bar left implementation preordained, while the present provisional adjudication does not have that established effect.
- Jefferson v. City of Tarrant supplies the distinction between a settled federal issue and a practically completed action. Its remaining liability, causation and claim proceedings supplied a distinct application of that rule.
- Florida v. Thomas supplies separate evaluation of each Cox route and effective later review; its express alternative-ground remand illustrates a different basis for substantive proceedings to continue.
- Johnson v. California, No. 03-6539, decided May 3, 2004, supplies the jurisdiction-invoking party's burden and rejects constitutional importance alone as an exception.
- Pennsylvania v. Ritchie retains its practical qualification: a merely conceivable later appeal does not establish useful later protection.

The parties remain in the state action under its existing preliminary-relief posture and may pursue the relief available there. Any renewed Supreme Court review must rest on its own judgment satisfying §1257 or an established practical-finality exception.

### 2005-03-02 — [Tenet v. Doe; No. 03-1395](../records/tenet-v-doe_merits_2005-03-02.md#public-projection)

A claim is barred when success on the pleaded right and requested relief necessarily requires judicial establishment of a secret espionage relationship with the Government and enforcement of the alleged undertaking arising from it. Estoppel or due-process labels do not remove that indispensable premise; a claim founded on an independent public legal duty or an applicable enacted enforcement channel must be assessed on its own source and requirements.

Reynolds's state-secrets privilege governs withholding evidence in an otherwise maintainable action; it does not replace Totten's distinct bar on maintaining an agreement-dependent suit. The Director's formal personal invocation required for that evidentiary privilege is therefore not a prerequisite to dismissing these claims under Totten, including their requests for interim support, administrative review and regulations.

Totten prevents the judicial inquiry necessary to maintain this category of action and may be applied at the threshold without first resolving the possible Tucker Act channel objection. This sequence rests on a prohibition against undertaking the inquiry, rather than a decision of ordinary contested liability on assumed jurisdiction.

**Reversed and remanded for dismissal, 9–0.**

**Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer.

The surviving estoppel and due-process claims founded on the alleged secret undertaking must be dismissed. The already dismissed equal-protection branch and denial of preliminary relief remain undisturbed.

- Totten v. United States retains its categorical prohibition on ordinary judicial enforcement necessarily dependent on a secret espionage undertaking; it applies to the agreement-dependent support and process claims here.
- United States v. Reynolds retains its evidentiary privilege and its formal-invocation requirements for that privilege. Its distinct function does not displace Totten's pleading-stage rule.
- Webster v. Doe retains constitutional review in its independent statutory employment setting; that acknowledged relationship differs from the secret undertaking necessary to these claims.
- Christopher v. Harbury supplies the requirement that an asserted injury and requested remedy connect to an actual underlying entitlement; it supplies no independent channel for the agreement-dependent claims.
- Citizens for a Better Environment v. Steel Co. retains its prohibition on contested merits adjudication upon assumed Article III power and its distinction for legitimate nonmerits thresholds.
- Ruhrgas AG v. Marathon Oil Co. supplies the absence of a universal hierarchy among threshold objections while preserving the jurisdiction-before-merits boundary.

The district court must identify the surviving agreement-dependent counts, dismiss them, and terminate further merits discovery on those claims. The mandate requires dismissal in place of the requested judicially ordered interim support, review procedure or regulations. Any properly sought amendment must identify an actual independent legal source and satisfy ordinary procedural requirements.


- **2005-03-07 — [Back v. Hastings on Hudson Union Free School District; judgment below, 365 F.3d 107.](../records/back-v-hastings-on-hudson_merits_2005-03-07.md#public-projection).** Ginsburg's unanimous opinion permits proof of intentional public-employment sex discrimination through supported sex-role statements and the whole record without a mandatory opposite-sex comparator. The employee retains persuasion by a preponderance and defendant-specific causation; the indirect framework shifts only production. Virginia supplied conduct-date notice against the alleged explicit maternal stereotypes in 2000–2001; later Hibbs, Hope and Brosseau explain current doctrine without creating earlier notice. Brennan and Wishnie face ordinary factfinding on the disputed conduct, motive and causation. The reviewed legal and immunity rulings are affirmed; the independent Russell and district judgments remain undisturbed.

- **2005-03-07 — [Ballard v. Commissioner; No. 03-184; consolidated with Estate of Kanter v. Commissioner, No. 03-1034.](../records/ballard-v-commissioner_merits_2005-03-07.md#public-projection).** Stevens's eight-Justice opinion reverses both Ballard and Estate of Kanter judgments. Tax Court Rule 183 applicable to these adjudications requires the identifiable original special-trial-judge report to remain in the judicial record, with party and appellate access and actual application of the prescribed weight to its supported findings. Collaborative revision cannot conceal or replace that operative report. The Tax Court must disclose it, allow a meaningful response and reconsider or explain the determination under the rule; an already compliant determination can be sustained. Neither a favorable initial report nor alteration of its actual findings is presumed. The mandate concerns both taxpayers and preserves lawful tax adjudication after the rule defect is addressed. Specific lawful confidentiality protections remain available while preserving meaningful party access and appellate review. The operative submitted report remains distinct from preliminary drafts and informal deliberations.

- **2005-03-07 — [Shepard v. United States; No. 03-9168.](../records/shepard-v-united-states_merits_2005-03-07.md#public-projection).** Thomas's Part I has six joins: Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. ACCA generic burglary following a broader plea must be established by conclusive judicial plea records, including the statute, charge, plea terms, colloquy or comparable express finding assented to by the defendant; unadopted police narratives cannot supply an admitted element. All nine join Part II's removal of the unconditional enhancement command under existing Gatewood and Booker law. Indispensable disputed recidivist facts require the applicable federal charge and reasonable-doubt element adjudication or valid admission and waiver; lawful jury waiver preserves the proof and charging boundaries. When an indispensable enhancement allegation is absent from the federal charge, the objection was preserved and no valid charging waiver supplies authority, sentencing notice and judicial findings cannot authorize enhancement; sentencing begins with the independently authorized range. For genuine forfeiture, Rule 52(b) requires error, plainness, the defendant's ordinary showing of outcome-affecting prejudice, and a separate finding that the error seriously affects the fairness, integrity or public reputation of judicial proceedings before discretionary correction. When settled trial law changes during direct review, plainness is assessed at review. Further enhancement proceedings require independent authority. Resentencing applies the retained binding Guidelines within the lawful range; the firearm conviction survives.

- **2005-03-07 — [Wilkinson v. Dotson; No. 03-287; claims of William Dwight Dotson and William Johnson.](../records/wilkinson-v-dotson_merits_2005-03-07.md#public-projection).** O'Connor's unanimous opinion affirms section 1983 availability for Dotson's and Johnson's parole-procedure claims because success would require lawful new proceedings while leaving discretion to deny release under valid sentences. The actual necessary legal effect of success controls; a procedural label or ultimate hope of release does not. Cavanaugh's opportunity-only rule, Heck and Edwards retain their distinct boundaries, and Muhammad and Nelson support compatible civil relief. Underlying due-process and ex-post-facto merits, ordinary procedural defenses and fitted prospective relief remain for adjudication. Kennedy joins on the concrete distinction between a corrected discretionary opportunity and correction of an invalid sentence authorizing custody.

- **2005-03-22 — [Black Political Task Force v. Galvin; lower-court No. 02-11190; 300 F. Supp. 2d 291.](../records/black-political-task-force-v-galvin_merits_2005-03-22.md#public-projection).** Souter's Part I is unanimous that the continuing permanent injunction leaves a live appeal despite enacted Plan C. Seven join Part II's section 2 framework: compact capacity through a lawful majority district or Valdespino's rigorous single-group dependable-crossover route, cohesion, ordinarily defeating bloc voting and independent preponderance proof under totality. Demonstrated crossover opportunity must count; preliminary conditions and proportionality create no presumption. Five join Part III affirming because supported six-precinct transfers, population needs, voting and the feasible alternative independently prove local packing despite the lower court's numerical and presumptive errors. The Count I declaration, seventeen-district injunction and statutory fee basis remain; Plan C is enacted state law, Counts II–III remain dismissed and completed 2004 candidate issues remain moot. Ordinary implementation and lawful modification continue. Candidate race is not voter preference; supported incumbency and unopposed-election circumstances receive case-specific weight. Proportional opportunity is relevant but neither a conclusive safe harbor nor a burden-shifting defense, and opportunity elsewhere cannot offset the proved local impairment. Any narrower injunction must be shown to cure that arrangement fully.

- **2005-03-22 — [Brown v. Payton; No. 03-1039; 544 U.S. 133.](../records/brown-v-payton_merits_2005-03-22.md#public-projection).** All nine join Breyer's Part I applying AEDPA to the post-enactment writ application and testing the November 23, 1992 merits adjudication against holdings established by that date. O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Breyer join Part II reversing the reviewed penalty-habeas relief: the complete qualified charge, repeated exclusion argument, substantial conversion evidence and competing advocacy permit the state conclusion within Boyde's objective statutory range. Admission of mitigation or defense advocacy is not independently a cure; the ruling gives neither categorical approval of factor (k) nor a new harmlessness rule. Statutory ineligibility suffices, leaving independent constitutional error and actual effect undecided by the majority. The convictions survive; unrelated habeas claims retain their lawful posture. The all-evidence direction was expressly subject to subsequent instructions, which included factor (k)'s crime-centered wording and weighing of applicable factors; the judge's private explanation was not delivered to the jury.

- **2005-03-22 — [Muehler v. Mena; No. 03-1423.](../records/muehler-v-mena_merits_2005-03-22.md#public-projection).** Ginsburg's unanimous Parts I/III permit occupant detention and reasonable initial cuffs during the weapons search, reject immigration-topic-only seizure liability, preserve separate post-search duration review and require verdict-sensitive relief. Questions adding neither custody nor a distinct coercive intrusion require no suspicion solely because of their subject; Caballes supplies no universal no-extra-time permission. Part II has five joins—Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer—requiring continued-force review under Graham and Holland with supported verdict facts, actual changing risks, control, harm, duration and supervision. The present questions do not support a separate immunity adjudication. Defenses properly remaining below retain preservation, finality and conduct-date notice rules. The unallocated compensation and separate punitive predicates require lawful analysis before retaining, reducing or retrying damages; rejected independent defense verdicts survive.

- **2005-03-22 — [Rancho Palos Verdes v. Abrams; No. 03-1601.](../records/rancho-palos-verdes-v-abrams_merits_2005-03-22.md#public-projection).** Thomas's unanimous opinion assumes the individual right and holds the City has shown that §332(c)(7)(B)(v)'s personal thirty-day expedited judicial action is incompatible with an ordinary parallel section 1983 action enforcing the same siting restrictions. Borrowing a period cannot create an unauthorized hybrid action. The saving clause preserves other law without requiring a second inconsistent route for the new entitlement; the distinct FCC-petition subject remains separate. This is demonstrated statutory incompatibility under Blessing, Sea Clammers and Smith, not a universal express-remedy presumption. Bruneau's compatible funding administration, Gonzaga's two-question framework and Sandoval/Powell prospective holdings remain. Dependent damages and section 1988 fees fail; the direct permit judgment survives. The complete direct-TCA damages question remains outside the presented channel issue. The FCC petition concerns the radio-frequency violation specified in clause (iv), whereas the competent-court action applies to clause (iii)'s written-decision and substantial-evidence obligation.

- **2005-03-29 — [City of Sherrill v. Oneida Indian Nation of New York; No. 03-855; the Lead, Eviction and Members cases at their reviewed scope.](../records/city-of-sherrill-v-oneida-indian-nation_merits_2005-03-29.md#public-projection).** Kennedy's Part I has seven joins: O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. After the extraordinary territorial dormancy, settled governance and ordinary reacquisition shown here, the Nation cannot revive sovereign property-tax immunity, affirmatively or defensively. This is an independent nonrevival qualification, not statutory taxability inferred from alienability; Yakima and Cass retain their actual federal-source and parcel-chain requirements. The Lead tax protection and corresponding laches-futility premise are reversed. The City's alternative reservation, diminishment and recognition theories remain undecided on the sufficient equity ground. All eight Associates join Part II; Stone-Zsela agrees only in its dispositions and adds no ground. The limitations, waiver, estoppel, in pari delicto and ratification amendment denials retain their separate grounds. The unsupported Rule 56(f) discovery request and acquisition-restraint request lacking irreparable harm remain denied. Independent Lead counterclaim, Eviction and Members immunity judgments survive, including the distinct Members official-capacity state-sales-tax damages dismissal; the unreviewed necessary-party alternative becomes no holding. Removing tax immunity supplies neither eviction authority nor tax-sale validation. Madison County retains its existing remand.

- **2005-03-29 — [Jackson v. Birmingham Board of Education; No. 02-1672; 544 U.S. 167.](../records/jackson-v-birmingham-board-of-education_merits_2005-03-29.md#public-projection).** Stevens's Parts I–III, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, reverse the categorical dismissal. A covered recipient intentionally discriminates on the basis of sex when it materially retaliates because a person reports or opposes sex discrimination in its educational program, including a personally injured advocate for others. Coverage, legally attributable recipient conduct, the sex-discrimination subject, materially adverse treatment, retaliatory causation and causal injury require proof. Cannon's established action reaches the statutory wrong; Sullivan's pre-enactment background and the longstanding regulation support construction and Pennhurst notice, with the duty grounded in the statute. Franklin permits appropriate compensation for proved intentional injury, while lawful prospective employment relief addresses continuing consequences. Gebser and Davis retain their distinct third-party attribution rules rather than impose a second-complaint prerequisite to the recipient's own decision. Powell, Sandoval and South Camden retain the actual bounded Title VI action. Jackson's coaching and pay allegations suffice at pleading; sequence alone does not compel causation, and the Board may establish a lawful explanation. Further proceedings must develop the decisionmaking and injury record and repair proved injury within lawful limits.

- **2005-03-29 — [Sabree ex rel. Sabree v. Richman; review of 367 F.3d 180 (3d Cir. 2004).](../records/sabree-ex-rel-sabree-v-richman_merits_2005-03-29.md#public-projection).** Scalia's unanimous Parts I–III affirm the Third Circuit's reversal of dismissal. Sections 1396a(a)(10) and (8), read with the operative section 1396d(a) payment definition and paragraph (15), give eligible beneficiaries rights to Pennsylvania's adopted intermediate-care assistance and reasonable promptness. The dated definition concerns payment of part or all covered costs, with its actual coverage timing, eligibility, clinical and facility conditions. The need determination, pre-admission or benefit-authorization care plan, independent review and active-treatment conditions operate at their statutory stages; eligible waiting-list claimants need not already receive the treatment allegedly withheld. Blessing supplies the provision-specific rights and separate foreclosure inquiries; Suter and the enacted plan corrections retain their scopes, and Gonzaga restates Blessing's factors. The individual section 1396a(a)(3) hearing and section 1396c funding mechanism, considered together, show no incompatibility with this section 1983 action. Under section 1396c, after reasonable notice and an opportunity for hearing, a finding that a changed plan no longer complies or administration fails substantially to comply requires notification and withholding until the Secretary is satisfied the failure will no longer occur; payments may be limited to unaffected categories or parts. Rancho's specific personal judicial action, thirty-day period and expedited adjudication remain distinct. The District Court must determine assistance owed, unreasonable delay, causation, official responsibility and lawful compliance. The financing-versus-furnishing issue remains for the developed record under the dated definition. Broader relief requires Rule 23 determinations and a proved common unlawful practice; any injunction must impose measurable lawful obligations, reasonable deadlines, adequate reporting and enforcement, with a narrower cure available if demonstrated.


**Completed chunk 6 controlling law.** The following public holdings, precedent treatment and resulting-law statements are copied from the completed Records; each statement retains the authority and qualifications its source supplies.

### 2005-03-30 — [California Public Employees’ Retirement System v. WorldCom, Inc.](../records/california-public-employees-retirement-system-v-worldcom-inc_merits_2005-03-30.md#public-projection)

**Holdings.**

#### The express nonremoval command governs these individual actions

**Holding and operative rule.** Section 1452(a) does not authorize removal of an individual Securities Act action filed in a state court of competent jurisdiction when §22(a), 15 U.S.C. §77v(a), prohibits its removal. Section 22(a)’s exception concerns §77p(c) removal of covered class actions based on state law that allege a material misstatement or omission, or manipulative or deceptive conduct, in connection with the purchase or sale of a covered security; the present individual actions fall outside that exception.

**Authority.** The Court’s opinion, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies the controlling rule and the review-channel holding below.

**Controlling explanation.** Congress’s broad bankruptcy-removal permission and its express prohibition for a defined class of securities cases can operate together. Things Remembered, Inc. v. Petrarca supplies that coexistence principle: bankruptcy procedure does not automatically displace independently applicable statutory limits. Section 1452(a) continues to authorize removal within its field; §22(a) prevents removal of these particular cases. A potential effect on a bankruptcy estate establishes, at most, a jurisdictional predicate rather than permission to disregard the forum command.

The absence from §1452(a) of §1441(a)’s introductory reservation for other Acts does not itself repeal §22(a). The removal provisions already differ in who may remove, what may be removed, and the jurisdictional conditions and exclusions. Section 22(a) addresses removal to any federal court. Its retained prohibition is not confined to §1441. The advantages of centralizing bankruptcy-related litigation cannot supply an exception Congress did not enact.

The statutory protection for actions brought by a State, political subdivision or state pension plan on its own behalf remains operative, as does its protection for an authorized class consisting solely of such named public entities. The entity-counting rule treats a pension plan or other qualifying entity as one person unless formed to participate in the action; its beneficiaries do not by themselves convert its individual case into a covered class action. On the individual-action status presented here, state-court return follows even assuming the asserted contribution, indemnification or reimbursement interests satisfy §1334. The actual sufficiency of the estate connection lies outside the certified statutory question, which assumes that connection.

#### The appeal concerns legal removal authority

**Holding and operative rule.** The accepted §1292(b) appeal presents the legal authority to remove these actions. Section 1452(b)’s bar on appellate review of a remand or refusal to remand on equitable grounds does not govern that distinct statutory question.

**Controlling explanation.** Quackenbush v. Allstate Insurance Co. requires attention to the ground of the remand decision and its lawful review channel. Things Remembered preserves the restrictions governing the kinds of remand it addresses. Here the order rejected the asserted statutory prohibition, and the certified appeal concerns that legal ruling. Section 1452(b) expressly protects both equitable remand and equitable refusal; the distinction rests on the ground of decision, not merely whether remand was granted or denied.

**Precedent Treatment.**

Things Remembered, Inc. v. Petrarca is applied for the coexistence of overlapping statutes and preserves its covered-remand review rule. Connecticut National Bank v.
