The facial Establishment ruling supplies no resolution of the separately preserved congressional-power defenses.

### 2005-05-31 — [Tory v. Cochran, No. 03-1488.](../records/tory-v-cochran_merits_2005-05-31.md#public-projection)

**Holdings.**

#### The continuing command preserves a live controversy

**Holding and operative rule:** When an injunction remains operative by its terms and is asserted to remain enforceable, the beneficiary's death does not eliminate the restrained speakers' live challenge merely because the original substantive interest has changed. The agreed substitution is permitted for this appeal, without determining a successor's independent entitlement to relief.

**Authority:** The Court's opinion is joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer. Those seven join this holding, the present-overbreadth holding and the corresponding vacatur and remand.

**Controlling explanation:** The command still regulates petitioners' speech, and respondent continues to claim its enforcement. A speaker need not risk contempt to obtain review of that burden. California procedures allowing later challenges do not make the current command disappear. Death changes the available justification and potential claimants; it does not, on this record, eliminate the restraint. The Court can correct that present injury without settling the independent state-law ownership, survival and successor-entitlement questions outside this appeal.

#### The present complete topic ban lacks a sufficient remedial justification

**Holding and operative rule:** In the present circumstances, the injunction's prohibition on all public statements about Cochran and his firm is an unjustifiably broad prior restraint. Cochran's death has eliminated or materially diminished its stated objective of preventing coercion of him into paying money, and the existing record supplies no new adjudicated justification for maintaining the same complete prohibition to protect the practice.

**Controlling explanation:** A prior restraint requires a justification fitted to the speech it forbids and the injury it prevents. Near, Keefe and Nebraska Press supply that protection against unsupported advance suppression. Madsen and Schenck require a remedial connection between wrongdoing and each restriction; their content-neutral formula does not itself validate this subject-based decree. Alexander distinguishes punishment for past conduct from an order actually forbidding future expression, which this injunction does.

The trial court's serious findings remain facts. They do not supply a current reason to suppress every truthful, unrelated or otherwise lawful statement after the central coercion-prevention objective has disappeared or materially diminished. An asserted interest in the firm cannot substitute for an adjudicated basis supporting this order's full reach. The continuing, express restraint also answers the argument for leaving all correction to California: present constitutional relief is available on the changed circumstances and existing record. State proceedings remain the place for any new supported remedy.

#### Relief follows the defect in the existing command

**Holding and operative rule:** The judgment sustaining the present public-topic prohibition is vacated, and its dependent enforcement must be removed with it. On remand an appropriate party may seek a different, precisely supported remedy for independently unlawful conduct under the applicable substantive and constitutional requirements.

**Controlling explanation:** Vacatur responds to the prospective restraint and preserves the lawful liability findings. A future decree must identify the party entitled to relief, the continuing wrong, the responsible persons and the speech or conduct properly restricted. Madsen and Schenck preserve the requirement of notice and actual concert before an injunction binds persons through concerted conduct; neither a general label nor the substitution makes the nonparty firm an original claimant. A prohibition on the same protected discussion cannot survive merely through a differently titled dependent clause. The constitutionality of a different injunction confined to adjudicated false statements requires an order and remedial record absent from this case; independently lawful remedies retain their own elements.

**Precedent Treatment.**

- **Near v. Minnesota:** Applied for the constitutional protection against unsupported advance suppression of publication.
- **Organization for a Better Austin v. Keefe:** Applied for the protection against an unsupported injunction suppressing public criticism.
- **Nebraska Press Association v. Stuart:** Applied for the demanding justification required for a prior restraint.
- **Madsen v. Women's Health Center:** Applied for provision-specific remedial fit and responsibility of the persons bound. Its content-neutral injunction standard is not a safe harbor for this topic-based prohibition.
- **Schenck v. Pro-Choice Network:** Applied for fitted restraints, notice and actual-concert limits, and removal of enforcement dependent on an invalid speech restriction.
- **Alexander v. United States:** Distinguished because this decree directly forbids future speech, whereas the prior-restraint classification there concerned punishment without a future-publication ban.

**Law After Decision.**

The existing complete public-topic restraint cannot survive its present lack of a sufficient remedial justification after Cochran's death. An operative judicial command asserted to remain enforceable is reviewable while it continues to burden the speakers. Relief removes that prohibition and dependent enforcement while preserving lawful liability and the opportunity for an appropriate party to seek a supported, constitutionally confined new remedy.

### 2005-05-31 — [Tucson Woman's Clinic v. Eden; review of 379 F.3d 531, amended August 23, 2004.](../records/tucson-womans-clinic-v-eden_merits_2005-05-31.md#public-projection)

**Holdings.**

#### Cumulative burdens require adjudication under the complete access standard

**Holding and operative rule:** Specific evidence of added expenditures, lost provider capacity and consequential delay precludes summary judgment for the State when it creates material disputes about attributable interference or justification under the complete previability framework. The lower court must examine each surviving obligation and the challenged interacting package, including supported affected subgroups, and apply the protections in their prescribed order.

**Authority:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer join the complete rule and additional mandate in the Court's opinion, supplying six direct joins. Kennedy separately joins the remand concerning protected choice, a substantial practical obstacle and timely necessary care; his seventh judgment vote does not join the residual severe- and meaningful-interference requirements. The six-Justice holding adopts the following complete operative standard:

**Current rule:** Before viability the patient decides whether to continue pregnancy without proving medical necessity or obtaining official approval of her reasons. The State may not prohibit that choice, confer effective control on another person, or prevent timely necessary life-and-health care. After viability, prohibition requires the life-and-health safeguard, including individualized physical and mental health considerations. Viability is individualized and medically supported, not a fixed constitutional week. Clinical appropriateness concerns safe and suitable care, not official approval of the patient's reasons; not every preference is a medical emergency. The following complete standard governs throughout previability:

1. **Identify the duty and protected baseline.** Identify the enacted command, authoritative construction, exceptions, enforcement consequences and relevant medical circumstances. Distinguish an actual obligation from assumed future administration. A previability prohibition, effective third-party veto or denial of timely necessary life-and-health care violates the baseline and cannot be rescued by justification. Necessary care must remain available before imminent death or catastrophic deterioration. Defining all noncompliant treatment as unsafe does not establish medical necessity.
2. **Identify actual function and purpose.** Identify what the obligation does and the asserted permissible objective, including safety, informed consent, truthful information, permissible parental involvement, health administration or financial accountability. The State may prefer childbirth and communicate truthful information. Moral disapproval does not independently justify obstruction, punishment of protected choice or burdens imposed for their deterrent effect. An obstructive purpose established by objective evidence of text, design, context and operation independently invalidates the requirement; a legislator's opposition to abortion alone does not establish that purpose. Voluntary persuasion differs from prevention through barriers. Evaluate each justification on its terms: medical claims need medical support, and deliberative claims need an explanation linking the particular obligation to informed decisionmaking.
3. **Establish actual interference, comparison and affected population.** Challengers must establish concrete additional interference caused by the challenged obligation. Relevant effects include extra appointments, material expense, consequential delay, loss of usable provider capacity, clinical risk, coercion and disclosure consequences affecting choice or access. Compare the actual system with and without the obligation while holding other relevant law and circumstances constant. For a challenged interacting package, identify the package and compare the system without it. Consider cumulative effects without counting the same burden twice. Define the affected population from the duty's operation before deciding constitutional injury, include evidence-supported subgroups, and keep the population stable. Do not average the burden across everyone pregnant or define the group as only those who prevail. Poverty, distance, transportation, employment and abuse matter insofar as they explain the State's added condition; identify that causal link. Exceptions, bypasses and alternative providers count only if actually usable. Meaningful interference means concrete, nontrivial additional burden on obtaining care or exercising the protected decision. It triggers review rather than establishing invalidity. No universal dollar, mileage or hourly threshold applies. Reliable clinical evidence, records, testimony and reasonable predictions can establish sufficiently demonstrated prospective injury before access is lost.
4. **Apply the substantive protections in order.** First, a baseline violation, unconstitutional obstructive purpose or substantial obstacle is independently invalid. A substantial obstacle seriously obstructs the affected population's practical opportunity for timely, clinically appropriate protected care; absolute impossibility is unnecessary, and success by some patients does not establish adequate access for others. A compelling interest cannot rescue an established obstacle. Second, remaining severe interference requires a compelling permissible justification and narrow tailoring. Identify the severe intrusion and explain why the independent prohibitions did not already decide the issue; overlapping protections require no artificial boundary, but an obstacle may not be relabeled as justifiable severe interference. Serious intrusion into clinical judgment, health or protected confidentiality may require this scrutiny despite usable access. Third, for remaining meaningful interference the State must substantiate a permissible justification and its fit to the added obligation. Abstract interests and conclusory assertions are insufficient. When challengers identify a concrete, practicable, substantially less burdensome alternative adequately serving the objective, the State must answer it; inadequacy, unavailability or material countervailing consequences may answer that showing. The State need not select the cheapest imaginable policy or disprove every hypothetical alternative. Reliable experience and reasonable predictions may suffice; particular legislative findings, a new study in every case, scientific certainty and numerical benefit calculations are not required. Once justification and fit are established, courts may not substitute their preferred policy. Below meaningful interference this heightened access inquiry ends, while prohibited-purpose claims, independent constitutional protections and ordinary lawful-regulation requirements remain available.
5. **Protect clinical judgment and truthful communication.** Evaluate medical justifications against competent evidence, accepted professional standards, the patient's circumstances and substantial contrary evidence. Substantial professional agreement receives serious evidentiary weight, without a professional association's veto or conclusive weight for a clinician's bare assertion or an official's unsupported preference. Necessary care must remain timely; unnecessary committee vetoes and documentation functioning as prior permission may not displace appropriate clinical judgment. Duties must be sufficiently definite and applicable fault requirements respected. Later reasonable medical disagreement alone does not establish every element of a punishable offense; lawful enforcement against fraud, malpractice and professional misconduct remains. Required medical information must be accurate, nonmisleading and material to informed consent. Concise factual assistance notices may inform choice without ideological assent; clinicians may explain limitations and correct misinformation. Accuracy does not alone validate burdensome timing, delivery or certification. Distinguish useful confidential oversight records from public disclosure and records enforcing invalid obligations.
6. **Identify the violation and tailor relief.** Specify the duty or application that fails, why, and the corresponding relief. Preserve independently lawful requirements under applicable severability law and expressly address dependent certifications, report fields and sanctions. An individual claim does not fail because others comply; broader relief requires a corresponding showing. An indivisible mandatory condition may be invalidated when its ordinary operation imposes demonstrated meaningful interference and the State fails to justify it. Voluntary compliance in some circumstances does not alone validate compulsory compliance. Otherwise confine relief to the established applications or groups. This qualification of a strict no-valid-applications approach does not remove proof of causation, scope or inadequate justification. For cumulative burdens, remedy the challenged obligation's or package's contribution without automatically invalidating all regulation. Replacing the trimester timetable does not dissolve injunctions or authorize enforcement of every challenged provision; the mandate must specify the resulting duties.

Challengers establish the duty, stable population, causation and triggering interference before the State bears the corresponding justification and fit burden. Gestational age may support a medically justified safety requirement, but a trimester boundary does not itself change the standard. Necessary-care protection includes competent individualized medical support for a material risk of death or serious health harm, including grave medically established mental danger, when the method is necessary for timely, safe and suitable care; preference or marginal comparative advantage alone is insufficient.

**Controlling explanation:** Casey supplies the whole rule; Lawall, Greenville and Taft preserve its independent operation for practical access and clinical protection. The providers offered concrete mechanisms: equipment expenses, nurse staffing, overtime and follow-up costs, predicted departures and loss of physician capacity, and resulting delay and clinical risk. These are evidence-supported forecasts requiring adjudication, not findings of completed closures. A general safety label and the absence of one exact predicted fee increase do not eliminate those disputes. Conversely, the providers' request for trial and the contested causation and justification record require factual proceedings under the rule.

The comparison must include the practical costs of surviving requirements, including privilege applications, meetings, on-call work and the actual on-site duty. The on-site privilege holder need not personally perform every procedure, but must remain until patients are stable and ready to leave recovery. Duties removed by the independent holdings cannot be counted as retained lawful obligations. The State's serious safety concerns receive their evidentiary force; they must answer the actual interference at the applicable level. The dissent's rational-regulation approach does not account for the protected constitutional choice governing this case, and limiting review to substantial obstacles would omit the framework's distinct protections for remaining severe and meaningful interference.

#### Ordinary provider categories and independent patient sex equality

**Holding and operative rule:** The professional category and exemption for very small first-trimester practices survive the presented equal-protection challenges because targeted safety oversight and administrative burden provide rational relationships to legitimate ends. The independent patient sex-equality claim must be adjudicated separately: an actual sex classification in terms or administration requires a genuine important objective and substantial relationship, while a genuinely neutral classification challenged for discrimination requires a choice made at least partly because of sex or its adverse consequences; disparity and awareness alone are insufficient.

**Authority:** All nine Justices join these results and Klinger’s classification-and-purpose rule. The direct common proposition requires no automatic classification of every abortion regulation as a sex distinction.

**Controlling explanation:** The law covers five or more first-trimester abortions in a month or any later-trimester abortion. Practice volume can rationally bear on regulatory expense and the scale of potential harm, and the profession is not a suspect class. Those conclusions do not answer the separately pleaded comparison of women's health services with comparably risky services for men. Klinger requires the court to identify the actual governmental distinction and relevant opportunity before choosing review. A medical label cannot extinguish that independent inquiry, and an actual sex classification does not require an additional finding of hostile motive before intermediate scrutiny applies. The lower court's rejection of particular animus evidence under rational review did not resolve the classification and comparator questions it treated as absorbed into access analysis. On remand the court must identify the actual classification or purposeful administration, make the required findings and apply the corresponding standard.

#### Immediate compulsory entry requires constitutionally adequate authority

**Holding and operative rule:** Arizona may not compel the challenged open-ended immediate clinic entry without a warrant or an established exception merely by newly imposing extensive regulation. The State must establish the exception on which it relies. In a genuinely closely regulated industry, Burger additionally requires a substantial governmental interest, necessity for warrantless inspection and a constitutionally adequate substitute constraining official discretion; this scheme does not establish those predicates for its full mandatory entry command.

**Authority:** All nine join the rule and remedy on Burger and protected-premises grounds. Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer also adopt Ferguson's operation-specific and no-general-waiver reasoning as corroboration.

**Controlling explanation:** Commercial medical premises retain constitutional protection. The newly imposed regulatory density cannot itself establish the historical regulated-industry premise or eliminate the privacy of examination rooms, patients and records. The operating-hours restriction supplies a time limit but not adequate control of the authorized intrusion. Legitimate urgent health oversight and professional licensure therefore do not sustain this breadth. Ordinary consent, an appropriately obtained warrant and a genuine exigency remain lawful sources for an inspection. Ferguson likewise requires attention to the actual medical operation and rejects treating attendance for care as general consent, while its coordinated prosecution setting differs from this administrative scheme. The restraint reaches compulsory entry without adequate authority and the applications and sanctions dependent on that entry command.

#### Recipient-specific limits govern department and contractor information access

**Holding and operative rule:** Official access to intimate patient information requires a sufficient connection among the information, actual recipient, regulatory function, scope, uses, retention and safeguards. The established department access and copying under R9-10-1511(A)(4)(b),(c), and APEX identified-ultrasound access and retention under §36-2301.02(B),(C), lack that connection and must be restrained at their unjustified scope.

**Authority:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer join the complete privacy rule, these applications and corresponding relief in the Court's opinion. Scalia and Thomas dissent from these two privacy dispositions.

**Controlling explanation:** Greenville, Lawall and Herring protect against unjustified authorized exposure, while allowing functionally justified identified audits, complaints and corroboration. Each identifier need not be indispensable. Here department access includes broad unredacted histories and copying without supported need-to-know restrictions; vague training assurances and uncertain sanctions do not adequately constrain its recipients and uses. APEX receives identified ultrasound prints with seven-year retention, minimal secure-storage terms for the first two years and inadequate later-storage and employee-access limits. A bare nondisclosure rule does not cure those permissions, and a proposed coding policy is not operative protection.

The constitutional injury concerns actual authorized access, not a presumption of unlawful leakage. Redaction, coding, consent and targeted follow-up are concrete alternatives when adequate to the task, rather than universal prerequisites. The dissent's constitutional-source objection cannot displace the recipient-specific protection established by Greenville and Lawall. The remedy must remove the proved excessive access and redistribution while preserving justified confidential oversight, with exact recipients, tasks and dependent consequences specified below.

#### Bounded serious-incident reports serve a distinct disciplinary function

**Holding and operative rule:** The limited report under R9-10-1504(B),(C) concerning an abortion-related death or serious injury to a patient or viable fetus may supply the patient's name, incident description and follow-up action to the responsible professional board under the distinct confidentiality protections of §§32-1451.01(C) and 32-1855.03(D). That defined disciplinary function, limited subject and protected disclosure provide the required connection on this record.

**Authority:** The same seven Justices join the complete privacy rationale. Scalia and Thomas join the rejection of this challenge and the permission for lawful bounded board reporting, giving the disposition nine votes without adding their joins to the substantive-privacy rationale.

**Controlling explanation:** A serious injury creates a concrete disciplinary inquiry for an identified professional board. The limited information and the board-specific protections materially distinguish that channel from department-wide access and contractor retention. Those other provisions' defects cannot be transferred to different recipients and safeguards. The amended appellate court entertained this particular issue and sustained the report; that ruling is affirmed at its actual scope.

#### Undefined dignity language cannot independently support punishment

**Holding and operative rule:** R9-10-1507's unconfined requirement of consideration, respect and full recognition of dignity and individuality cannot independently support civil, criminal or adverse licensing sanctions without an ascertainable operative standard. Relief removes that independently punishable branch and dependent consequences while preserving definite clinical and professional duties.

**Authority:** All nine Justices join this confined rule and remedy.

**Controlling explanation:** Greenville distinguishes contextual professional standards from undefined additional duties selected by administrators. The value of respectful care does not identify the additional conduct required for full recognition of dignity and individuality. This record supplies no authoritative limitation or recognized clinical criterion that makes an independent sanction ascertainable. A warning, mitigation or promise of careful enforcement cannot create the omitted standard. The defect differs from a recognizable professional-core obligation: it concerns punishment based on an inspector's or complainant's subjective judgment under this abstract additional requirement. Removing that sanction branch leaves voluntary aspirations and independently ascertainable care requirements intact.

#### Constrained hospital qualifications do not establish the alleged private veto

**Holding and operative rule:** The facial standardless-delegation challenge fails where Arizona law supplies hospital-related legitimate standards, notice and hearing, and judicial review for reasonableness and substantial evidentiary support, and the record establishes no actual or supported impending arbitrary refusal based on abortion practice. The costs and operational burdens of the qualification requirement remain part of the access proceedings at the scope described above.

**Authority:** All nine Justices join the qualification ruling and rejection of the present delegation claim. The access consequences retain the six-Justice complete-framework and Kennedy narrower joins already stated.

**Controlling explanation:** Greenville permits objective professional qualifications and emergency-care arrangements while forbidding arbitrary, legally uncontrolled private licensing consent. Arizona's Holmes, Peterson and Blende rules provide actual constraints and review. The possibility of private discretion does not establish an uncontrolled veto under this record and those enforceable limits. A supported impending arbitrary exclusion can be challenged before a clinic closes; public-hospital restrictions cannot simply be assumed to govern every private institution. The present rejection concerns the private-control theory. Staffing, applications, meetings, on-call duties and the on-site requirement's effects still must be considered under the governing access rule.

#### Remedy follows the precise violation and its proved scope

**Holding and operative rule:** The lower court must apply Arizona severability law to the identified unconstitutional duties and applications, remove their dependent enforcement consequences, preserve independently lawful obligations, and promptly specify the stipulated restraint's continuing operation. Further factual and equitable determinations must identify the actual violation, affected population and scope before selecting corresponding relief.

**Authority:** All nine join those common severability and implementation requirements for the independent defects they support. The complete-framework majority of six joins their application to all access grounds; Kennedy joins effective relief on protected-choice, obstacle, necessary-care and independent grounds; Scalia and Thomas retain their stated objections to relief resting on abortion or substantive informational privacy.

Once a common-policy violation and its scope are proved, the seven Justices supporting the protected-choice/necessary-care and independent privacy grounds also apply Taft's corrective-policy rule: relief must protect the governed patients and providers unless the State establishes that a narrower remedy fully cures the violation. Six join its application when the predicate is the residual access framework. Challengers retain the initial burden to prove injury and scope.

**Controlling explanation:** The unresolved access and patient-equality questions require further adjudication before a final severability package can be fixed. The stipulation remains governed by its terms pending a prompt reasoned order; partial affirmance does not itself authorize blanket enforcement. The court must identify retained duties, prohibited applications, bound officials and dependent civil, criminal and licensing consequences. Any continuation or modification of protection requires the existing stipulation and ordinary equitable predicates. Taft's application of Anderson requires a remedy matching a proved common-policy injury, while preserving the State's opportunity to establish a complete narrower cure. The court should expedite genuinely material factual work and state the resulting obligations precisely.

**Precedent Treatment.**

- **Casey:** Applied in full. Its protected choice, individualized viability, timely necessary care, six-step sequence, proof allocation and tailored-remedy rules govern the access claim.
- **Lawall:** Applied for independent access review and the recipient-specific connection required for intimate-information transfer; necessary confidential handling differs from unjustified outside access.
- **Greenville:** Applied for independent access review and the recipient/function/scope/use/safeguard privacy rule, permitting justified identified oversight without universal indispensability. Its notice and qualification holdings preserve ascertainable professional duties and objective qualifications while forbidding undefined independent sanctions and arbitrary uncontrolled private licensing consent. Arizona's different duties and safeguards receive their own application.
- **Women's Medical Professional Corp. v. Taft:** Applied for the complete framework, competent individualized necessary-care protection and correction of a proved common-policy violation. Its Ohio-specific statutory and clinical applications remain distinct from Arizona's duties.
- **Klinger:** Applied to require classification-specific equality review and to distinguish actual sex classifications from purpose-based challenges to genuinely neutral administration. The rationality of professional categories cannot absorb the independent patient claim.
- **United States v. Virginia:** Supplies the same demanding important-objective/substantial-relationship standard for actual sex exclusion to the seven Justices joining its application; it supplies no automatic classification of every reproductive regulation.
- **New York v. Burger:** Applied for protected commercial premises and the actual predicates and discretion limits of warrantless inspection in a closely regulated industry.
- **Ferguson:** Supplies the seven-Justice corroborating operation-specific/no-waiver principle; its prosecution facts are distinguished.
- **Herring:** Applied, with Lawall and Greenville, to recipient-specific protection against unjustified onward exposure of intimate medical information.
- **Anderson v. Spear:** Applied through Taft for correction at the scope of a proved common-policy violation, with a narrower complete cure available on the State's showing.

**Law After Decision.**

The licensing scheme is subject to separate access, equality, search, privacy, notice and private-control requirements. Concrete prospective cost, capacity and delay evidence receives factual adjudication under the complete Casey framework; surviving professional categories and qualification rules remain subject to that independent access review. The patient sex-equality claim receives its own classification-and-purpose inquiry.

The established compulsory-entry command, excessive department/APEX permissions and undefined independent dignity sanctions must be restrained at their unconstitutional scope. Lawful inspections, fitted confidential oversight, the bounded serious-incident reports, definite professional duties and the qualification rule remain available under their governing conditions. Arizona-law severability, the remaining factual proceedings and the operative stipulation determine the resulting enforceable package and protection.

### 2005-06-06 — [Gonzales v. Raich, No. 03-1454.](../records/gonzales-v-raich_merits_2005-06-06.md#public-projection)

**Holdings.**

#### Local production and possession are integral to this interstate supply regime

**Holding and operative rule:** Congress may include unauthorized local marijuana cultivation, caregiver supply and possession in the Controlled Substances Act's independently valid interstate drug-control regime because that inclusion is reasonably adapted and integral to control of the same fungible substance's supply and distribution. The Government must identify the valid interstate regime, enacted covered class and genuine functional relationship; courts independently determine those boundaries while respecting reasonable congressional enforcement judgments within them.

**Authority:** The opinion of the Court, joined by all nine Justices, controls this necessary-inclusion rule, its application and the following statutory-boundary and remedial holdings.

**Controlling explanation:** Lopez requires an eligible interstate connection before aggregation. Home Builders supplies the necessary-inclusion route, and Gibbs, Rancho Viejo and GDF apply it without demanding border crossing, commercial motive or a separate substantial interstate effect for each local member of a genuinely connected class. Those decisions supply the rule, not facts about drugs.

The CSA controls production, distribution and possession through an integrated supply-and-authorization system for the substance traded interstate. Cultivation creates that substance, caregiver delivery transfers it, and possession retains it. Local unauthorized supply and its substitution for market acquisition bear directly on the regime's objects and enforceability. Section 801's supply and practical-differentiation findings support that concrete mechanism; generalized health costs or the description of the Act as comprehensive cannot supply the constitutional connection by themselves. The accepted local cultivation and possession remain within the mechanism even without a sale or interstate movement by these patients. The rule preserves independent judicial scrutiny of an application outside the enacted class, an invalid interstate regime or a nominal or pretextual relationship. It requires no least-restrictive-means showing or proof that every conceivable exemption would cause measurable enforcement failure.

#### Medical purpose and state permission do not remove the functional connection

**Holding and operative rule:** The accepted local, unpaid, physician-recommended and state-permitted setting does not sever the relationship between these production and possession activities and the federal substance-control system. California permission and a physician's recommendation do not amend federal authorization; the CSA's enacted definitions, authorization provisions and state-law limits retain their actual operation.

Section 841(a) prohibits knowing or intentional manufacture, distribution, dispensing and the related possession-with-intent conduct except as authorized by the subchapter. Section 844(a) governs knowing or intentional possession, preserving substances obtained directly, or under a valid prescription or order, from a practitioner acting professionally, and other authorization under the applicable subchapters. Those are federal statutory conditions, rather than an exemption created by state permission alone.

Section 802 includes planting, cultivation, growing and harvesting in production. Distribution is delivery other than administering or dispensing; delivery includes actual, constructive or attempted transfer regardless of an agency relationship and does not require payment. The manufacture definition excludes preparation, compounding, packaging or labeling in conformity with state or local law by a practitioner as an incident to professional administration or dispensing; that limited exception is not a general cultivation exemption. Marijuana remains governed by Schedule I and the federal authorization, research, registration and scheduling routes in force. Section 903 leaves otherwise valid state law in place unless a positive conflict prevents the federal provision and state law from consistently standing together. Direct federal regulation of private conduct does not require California to criminalize the same conduct or administer the federal program.

**Controlling explanation:** The patients' strongest boundary would define a separately administered local medical class and require proof that excluding it would defeat interstate enforcement. Their medical needs, lack of sales and state supervision are accepted at their proper record status. But medical purpose explains why production or possession occurs; it does not change the substance produced or remove the activity from the authorization and supply controls. A State's ability to administer an exception may inform the functional inquiry without obliging Congress to adopt that State's alternative system. The Court need not find patient-specific diversion or failure of California safeguards to identify this direct relationship. Household substitution alone would be too broad; the holding depends on the actual class and same-substance supply mechanism within an independently valid regime. The statutory operation and accepted activity facts settle that relationship here, so a further Commerce evidentiary proceeding is unnecessary.

#### Independent patient claims retain their own adjudication

**Holding and operative rule:** The preliminary-injunction direction cannot stand on the rejected Commerce theory. The case returns for genuinely preserved independent patient claims and any request for interim relief under the substantive law, evidence and equitable requirements governing those claims.

**Controlling explanation:** Oakland Cannabis rejected the distributor's statutory medical-necessity exemption and an equitable recreation of it, while leaving individual possession, patient necessity and independent constitutional claims outside that disposition. The present grant resolves federal power, rather than the distinct due-process, Ninth Amendment, patient-necessity or commandeering theories. Those matters remain because they fall outside the question decided and require their own legal and factual adjudication. The physicians' evidence keeps its proper significance for medical risk and any applicable claim or interim-relief requirement; the Commerce decision does not decide those claims' merits or guarantee relief. The lower courts must address the actual preserved requests promptly under their proper standards.

**Precedent Treatment.**

- **United States v. Lopez:** Applied to require an identifiable connection fitted to the enacted class before aggregation; generalized national health, crime or productivity consequences remain insufficient substitutes.
- **National Association of Home Builders v. Babbitt:** Applied at its reasonably-adapted and integral necessary-inclusion scope, with independent judicial identification of the valid regime, actual class and genuine relationship.
- **Gibbs v. Babbitt:** Applied for class-based inclusion without individual border crossing, commercial motive or a separate substantial interstate effect.
- **Rancho Viejo, LLC v. Norton:** Applied for the genuine regime/class/relationship inquiry and its outside-class, invalid-regime and nominal-or-pretextual limits.
- **GDF Realty Investments, Ltd. v. Norton:** Applied for the same necessary-inclusion rule and rejection of a routine member-specific effects hearing when the actual statutory relationship is established. Its wildlife facts do not establish this drug relationship.
- **United States v. Oakland Cannabis Buyers' Cooperative:** Preserved at its distributor-specific statutory and equitable scope, including its individual-patient and independent constitutional reservations.

**Law After Decision.**

The existing necessary-inclusion rule permits the CSA to reach unauthorized local medical cultivation, caregiver supply and possession within its genuine same-substance interstate control system. State permission and absence of payment do not create the claimed Commerce exemption. Courts must continue to identify a valid regime, enacted class and actual functional connection; statutory authorization and the distinct preserved patient claims retain their own requirements.

### 2005-06-06 — [Alaska v. United States; No. 128, Original.](../records/alaska-v-united-states_exceptions_2005-06-06.md#public-projection)

A State claiming additional submerged lands as historic inland waters must establish a continuous assertion of authority to exclude foreign navigation, including innocent passage, with foreign acquiescence. Alaska's evidence fails to establish that status for the disputed Alexander Archipelago pockets and enclaves beyond the ordinary coastal and island belts.

An asserted juridical bay must satisfy Article 7(2)'s actual geographic requirements: a well-marked indentation whose penetration in relation to its mouth creates landlocked waters and more than a mere coastal curvature, together with the separate semicircle-area condition. Even assuming Alaska's proposed assimilation of four islands into a constructive peninsula, its proposed North Bay and South Bay fail the well-marked-indentation requirement.

Federal retention of prestatehood submerged lands requires both clear inclusion in the reservation and clear intent to retain federal ownership through statehood. Glacier Bay's established inclusion and the Alaska Statehood Act's treatment of this wildlife reservation satisfy those distinct requirements, so the United States retains the identified submerged lands.

The sovereign parties must submit a proposed decree using the Master's established geographic descriptions and the federal disclaimer of the Tongass claim. The decree must reflect federal title to the adjudicated Archipelago pockets and Glacier Bay lands and resolve the disclaimed controversy consistently with that disclaimer.

The ordinary Convention coastline and state coastal/island belts remain the starting point. Additional historic-water title requires the established sovereignty, continuity and acquiescence showing. Proposed juridical bays must meet the Convention's geographic criteria even when island assimilation is assumed. For Glacier Bay, the actual wildlife reservation and Statehood Act establish federal retention through admission under the separate clear-inclusion and clear-retention requirements.

### 2005-06-06 — [Spector v. Norwegian Cruise Line Ltd.; No. 03-1388.](../records/spector-v-norwegian-cruise-line-ltd_merits_2005-06-06.md#public-projection)

Foreign registry alone does not exempt a cruise operator's public accommodations and covered transportation in United States waters from Title III of the Americans with Disabilities Act. Each passenger claim must be assessed under its particular statutory duty and any applicable maritime limitation.

Disability-screening eligibility criteria require necessity to the offered service; necessary reasonable policy modifications remain subject to fundamental alteration. Necessary auxiliary aids retain their distinct fundamental-alteration and undue-burden defenses; barrier removal, and alternative access when removal is not readily achievable, retain their own readily-achievable conditions.

A particular Title III obligation regulating a foreign ship's genuinely internal affairs requires the congressional clarity appropriate to that interference. The application-specific limit reaches a permanent and significant alteration of basic ship design or construction that interferes with internal maritime affairs; it does not exempt the ship's unrelated passenger services.

The categorical foreign-flag judgment is reversed and the claims remanded for adjudication under the applicable duties, defenses and maritime rule. The district court's distinct reliance on absent ship-specific design regulations remains for appellate consideration because the Fifth Circuit did not reach it.

Foreign-flag status alone does not defeat domestic Title III passenger claims. The statute supplies distinct duty-specific qualifications, including its applicable feasibility, fundamental-alteration, undue-burden and safety limits. Five Justices adopt the confined application-specific maritime rule for genuinely internal affairs; six require further adjudication of structural claims.

### 2005-06-06 — [United States v. Kincade; review of 379 F.3d 813, Ninth Circuit en banc.](../records/united-states-v-kincade_merits_2005-06-06.md#public-projection)

A qualifying conviction and a supervised-release condition do not by themselves authorize compulsory biological sampling and protected examination for a general criminal-investigation database. The government must establish individualized suspicion together with lawful search authority, valid consent, or a constitutionally sufficient exception whose purpose and scope fit the search; it has established none for Kincade's demand.

The lower courts must remove the unconstitutional collection requirement and any consequence attributable solely to refusal, correct the operative release judgment, and provide effective relief from its continuing effects. Because collection actually occurred, the district court must address the appropriate disposition of the obtained material and database profile under this acquisition ruling and ordinary remedial law, including deletion and return or destruction necessary to cure the violation, subject to an independently established lawful basis.

The investigative DNA program cannot derive sufficient search authority from conviction coverage and supervision alone. Rise governs the bodily acquisition, with the actual authority, consent or constitutionally sufficient exception required for the search. Courts correcting unconstitutional refusal enforcement must separate dependent consequences from independent grounds and address continuing material or profile effects through effective relief on the actual claims.

### 2005-06-13 — [Bradshaw v. Stumpf, No. 04-637; 545 U.S. 175.](../records/bradshaw-v-stumpf_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

A guilty plea is knowing as to an essential offense element when the record establishes that competent counsel adequately explained the element and the defendant understood it; the judge need not personally give a prescribed recital. Stumpf’s counsel’s express representation and his own confirmation establish understanding here, and his denial that he fired the fatal shots does not contradict purposeful aggravated-murder complicity under Ohio law.

The State’s later presentation identifying Wesley as the shooter does not invalidate Stumpf’s plea to purposeful aggravated-murder complicity. Shooter identity was not essential to the admitted offense, and the later presentation does not establish that Stumpf’s earlier assent lacked the required understanding.

An informed aggravated-murder plea does not dispose of a claim that materially false or irreconcilable prosecution accounts affected the defendant’s individual culpability in the selection of death. The reviewing court must separately identify the actual accounts and later evidence, the State’s knowledge, their sentencing use, the applicable constitutional ground, and material prejudice under the governing pre-AEDPA standards.

Counsel-provided explanation and the whole plea record may establish understanding of an essential element, while actual misunderstanding remains constitutionally material. Purposeful complicity and personal shooter identity remain distinct. A surviving conviction does not dispose of a preserved independent claim that the State’s factual accounts impaired the individualized selection of death.

### 2005-06-13 — [Golden v. Golden; Third Circuit No. 03-2184; 382 F.3d 348. No separate Supreme Court docket is established.](../records/golden-v-golden_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

The probate exception reserves probate or annulment of a will, administration of an estate, and control of property in a state court’s custody. Otherwise proper personal claims—including fraud, undue influence, forgery and fiduciary-duty claims—are not excluded merely because a trust functions as a will substitute or deciding them could produce facts inconsistent with a probate determination; a damages label cannot authorize the reserved acts.

Earwood’s personal slander claim and both claimants’ intentional-interference-with-inheritance claims remain available for ordinary adjudication. Dismissals of the undue-influence, fraud, forgery and fiduciary-duty counts are vacated insofar as they rest on general estate interference; the district court must retain jurisdictionally proper personal claims while excluding actual probate, estate-administration and property-control demands.

A bona fide diversity claim satisfies the amount requirement when legally available, nonfrivolous punitive damages prevent a legal-certainty conclusion that recovery falls below the statutory threshold. Each claimant’s allegations here satisfy §1332, considering that claimant’s permitted claims and punitive damages without combining different plaintiffs’ demands or counting mutually exclusive recoveries twice.

A power of attorney does not substitute the agent’s citizenship for that of the person whose beneficial claim is asserted; §1332(c)(2) attributes the decedent’s citizenship to an estate’s legal representative. Leah Golden’s New York citizenship and Helen Earwood’s Georgia citizenship are diverse from the Pennsylvania defendants.

Section 1738 requires the rendering State’s proper preclusive effect for an actual state adjudication. An inferred acceptance of an instrument or a filed valuation cannot supply a blanket dismissal without identifying what was actually adjudicated and the applicable preclusion predicates.

The Rooker–Feldman bar applies when a state-court loser brings a later federal action complaining of injury caused by the state judgment and seeking its review and rejection. A personal claim based on the defendants’ independent misconduct does not acquire that appellate character merely because a factual conclusion could conflict with a state determination.

A trust’s testamentary function and factual overlap with probate do not themselves exclude diverse personal claims. Federal courts must identify the actual act and remedy requested, reserve probate and estate-property control to the proper state forum, and adjudicate otherwise proper personal claims under state substantive law. Citizenship, the sufficient pleaded amount, actual state-appellate-review predicates and §1738 effects retain their separate functions.

### 2005-06-13 — [Grable & Sons Metal Products, Inc. v. Darue Engineering & Manufacturing, No. 04-603; 545 U.S. 308.](../records/grable-and-sons-v-darue-engineering_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

A state-law claim arises under 28 U.S.C. §1331 when the well-pleaded claim necessarily raises an actually disputed federal issue substantial to the federal system and federal adjudication is consistent with the allocation of judicial business Congress established. Grable’s quiet-title claim satisfies all four conditions, permitting removal under §1441(a), although federal law supplies no separate cause of action.

Federal courts may hear the small class of state claims satisfying necessity, actual dispute, importance to the federal system, and consistency with Congress’s allocation of judicial responsibilities. The party seeking the federal forum must establish those conditions. A missing separate federal cause of action is relevant within that complete inquiry; merely borrowing a federal standard in an ordinary fact-bound state tort does not establish jurisdiction.

### 2005-06-13 — [Johnson v. California, No. 04-6964; 545 U.S. 162.](../records/johnson-v-california-batson_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

A defendant satisfies the first step of Batson by presenting facts sufficient to permit an inference of purposeful racial discrimination; the defendant need not first establish that discrimination was more likely than not. Striking all three qualified Black prospective jurors from the forty-three-person eligible venire supplies that inference in the circumstances here, requiring the prosecutor’s explanations and an ultimate purpose finding on which Johnson retains the burden of persuasion.

After an erroneous refusal to require explanations, the state court must promptly determine whether a reliable Batson adjudication remains possible, obtain the prosecutor’s actual reasons, permit meaningful challenge, and make the ultimate purpose finding. If purposeful discrimination is established, or a reliable adjudication cannot cure the omitted inquiry, the conviction must be set aside and a lawful new trial provided.

A supported inference of racial discrimination requires the strike proponent to explain the challenged peremptory strikes. Final persuasion remains with the objector after the reasons are tested. Courts reviewing a failure to conduct that inquiry must ensure a reliable adjudication or the new-trial correction the holding requires.

### 2005-06-13 — [Merck KGaA v. Integra Lifesciences I, Ltd., No. 03-1237; 545 U.S. 193.](../records/merck-kgaa-v-integra-lifesciences_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

Section 271(e)(1) protects the enumerated acts solely for uses reasonably related to developing and submitting information under the covered federal regulatory law. For the compound uses presented here, that relationship exists at least where the drugmaker reasonably believes the compound may act through an identified biological process to produce the intended physiological effect and uses it in experiments that, if successful, would yield information appropriate for FDA submission; the party invoking the defense must establish the relationship for each challenged use as of the time it occurred.

Failure to follow good-laboratory-practice rules does not categorically defeat §271(e)(1) protection for the preclinical work at issue. Those rules govern safety studies rather than efficacy, mechanism, pharmacology or pharmacokinetics studies as such, and the IND provision permits a covered safety study to be submitted with a brief explanation of noncompliance; the actual experiment must still satisfy the reasonable regulatory relationship.

The Federal Circuit must review Merck’s preserved challenge to the verdict under the correct construction and the admissible trial record. The consented-to jury instruction was consistent with the governing reasonable-relationship rule; correction of the appellate premises requires renewed review of evidentiary sufficiency and the resulting liability and damages consequences, rather than automatic judgment exempting every challenged experiment.

Qualifying preclinical safety, efficacy, mechanism, pharmacology and pharmacokinetics experiments receive the statutory protection on an objectively reasonable regulatory relationship established for each challenged use. That relationship is judged when the use occurs. Failure of a candidate or omission from an ultimate submission does not itself defeat protection, while a merely speculative pharmaceutical prospect supplies no exemption. Courts must apply the rule to the preserved evidence and then identify the actionable uses and resulting damages.

### 2005-06-13 — [Miller-El v. Dretke, No. 03-9659; 545 U.S. 231.](../records/miller-el-v-dretke_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

A state finding of nondiscriminatory jury selection rests on an unreasonable factual determination under §2254(d)(2) when the full lawful state-court record makes the prosecutor’s actual explanations untenable. Here the permitted record separately rebuts the relevant factual finding by clear and convincing evidence under §2254(e)(1) and establishes purposeful racial exclusion under Batson.

Courts applying Batson on habeas must assess the prosecutor’s actual explanations against the cumulative lawful record and consider materially comparable accepted jurors without demanding identity in every respect. Material differences remain part of the comparison. This record establishes purposeful exclusion, objective factual unreasonableness and clear-and-convincing rebuttal as distinct conclusions, supporting conditional trial relief.

### 2005-06-13 — [Wilkinson v. Austin, No. 04-495; 545 U.S. 209.](../records/wilkinson-v-austin_merits_2005-06-13.md#public-projection)

State law creates a protected prison liberty interest when it meaningfully restricts officials’ authority to impose a significant deprivation. Ohio’s genuine substantive placement predicates, combined with OSP’s exceptional isolation, indefinite duration and loss of otherwise available parole eligibility, establish that interest in this confinement.

For the OSP classification at issue, due process is satisfied by usable notice of the actual placement grounds, a meaningful opportunity for oral and written rebuttal, stated reasons and multilevel review in which a negative recommendation terminates placement, objections before final review, review within thirty days after arrival, and genuine annual reconsideration. Those protections make the New Policy adequate without the additional mandatory witness procedures imposed below; security-based restrictions must preserve a meaningful opportunity to contest the actual grounds.

The injunction must conform to the New Policy’s constitutional adequacy while promptly correcting any proved continuing OSP placement that has not received the required process. Each retained command must be necessary to correct the established federal violation, narrowly drawn and the least intrusive necessary means, with substantial weight given to public safety and criminal-justice effects; relief departing from state or local law additionally requires that federal law demand the departure, that it be necessary, and that no other relief correct the violation.

Ohio’s constrained OSP assignment implicates protected liberty under Sandin. The New Policy’s functioning combination of notice, rebuttal, reasons and recurring review supplies due process in this setting. Continuing prison relief must correct proved injury through the specific statutory conditions and workable implementation; the adequacy of the designed policy and the correction of deficient continuing placements require distinct determinations.

### 2005-06-20 — [American Trucking Associations, Inc. v. Michigan Public Service Commission, No. 03-1230](../records/american-trucking-associations-v-michigan-public-service-commission_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

An evenhanded annual per-truck fee for undertaking a distinct local hauling business does not violate the Commerce Clause merely because carriers performing fewer local miles pay more per mile. Courts must examine the actual charged event, discriminatory operation, fair apportionment and relation to state services, and any interstate burden clearly excessive relative to local benefits; Michigan’s §478.2(1) fee satisfies those requirements on this record.

The existing Commerce Clause framework permits an evenhanded charge for each distinct local hauling business when its actual operation meets the independent constitutional requirements. A higher per-mile cost caused solely by fewer local miles does not establish discrimination. A carrier’s additional local businesses in other States are separate charged events; courts must still examine the practical burden and relation to local regulation.

### 2005-06-20 — [Dodd v. United States, No. 04-5286](../records/dodd-v-united-states_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

Under §2255 paragraph 6(3), the one-year limitation runs from the Supreme Court’s initial recognition of the asserted right, provided the right is newly recognized and made retroactively applicable on collateral review. A later retroactivity determination does not restart that year; Richardson initially recognized the right Dodd asserts on June 1, 1999, making his April 4, 2001 motion untimely under this trigger even assuming its retroactive qualification.

For initial and successive §2255 motions invoking paragraph 6(3), courts calculate the year from initial Supreme Court recognition and separately test the new-right and retroactivity qualifications. A later retroactivity decision supplies no restart. The latest-event structure continues to permit another statutory trigger only when that trigger’s own conditions are met; successive-motion authorization remains an additional requirement.

### 2005-06-20 — [Graham County Soil & Water Conservation District v. United States ex rel. Wilson, No. 04-169](../records/graham-county-soil-water-conservation-district-v-united-states-ex-rel-wilson_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

The six-year period measured by §3731(b)(1) from a violation of §3729 does not govern the independent retaliation action created by the operative 1986 version of §3730(h). Absent another applicable federal period, courts borrow the most closely analogous state limitation under the established borrowing rules, selecting it by the substantive character of the retaliatory injury and determining accrual and any supported tolling under the applicable rules.

Courts adjudicating retaliation claims under the operative §3730(h) must use the appropriate borrowed state period, with accrual and any supported tolling determined under the applicable rules, rather than §3731(b)(1)’s fraud-triggered six years. Selection follows the character of the retaliatory injury and federal borrowing principles. The cause retains McKenzie’s independent protection even where no completed fraud is proved; the separate fraud action remains governed by its own limitations rules.

### 2005-06-20 — [Rompilla v. Beard, No. 04-5462](../records/rompilla-v-beard_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

Under the unreasonable-application branch of 28 U.S.C. 2254(d)(1), relief from a state merits decision requires an objectively unreasonable application of the Supreme Court law established at that adjudication, rather than merely an incorrect application. Pennsylvania’s December 10, 1998 approval of this investigation unreasonably applied Strickland because the other inquiries did not supply a reasonable basis for omitting adequate examination of the known accessible source the prosecution intended to use substantially in seeking death.

Capital counsel must examine a readily available prior-case file when counsel knows the prosecution will use its contents as a substantial part of the case for death, unless a reasonable, informed professional judgment makes that examination unnecessary. That duty requires reasonable efforts in the actual circumstances and reasonable pursuit of material leads; counsel’s inadequate timely examination of this source fell below Strickland’s objective standard.

Where deficient investigation would have materially changed the mitigation inquiry and presentation, prejudice exists when the supported cumulative evidence, weighed with the full aggravation case and its own adverse aspects, creates a reasonable probability of a different lawful penalty. That probability undermines confidence in Rompilla’s death sentence; because the state court did not adjudicate prejudice, the Court decides this element independently on the developed record.

Pennsylvania must provide an adequately prepared new penalty proceeding within a definite reasonable period set by the District Court or impose a lawful noncapital sentence. The District Court must require notice of the State’s chosen course, verify completion through the resulting sentencing order, and enforce noncompliance against the invalid death sentence and its execution while preserving the conviction and independently lawful custody.

The established Strickland rule requires reasonable examination of an accessible prior-case source known to supply substantial aggravation, absent a reasonable informed basis for omission. Other investigative work does not by itself answer that obligation. In federal habeas review, the state-adjudication statutory threshold remains separate from actual deficiency and prejudice; an unadjudicated prejudice component is assessed independently after the threshold is satisfied. The whole lawful penalty case, including unfavorable material, governs reasonable probability. The remedy corrects the sentence while preserving the conviction.

### 2005-06-20 — [San Remo Hotel, L.P. v. City and County of San Francisco, No. 04-340](../records/san-remo-hotel-v-city-and-county-of-san-francisco_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

A takings claimant’s compliance with a state-compensation prerequisite and reservation of federal causes do not exempt issues actually and necessarily decided by the state court from the rendering State’s lawful preclusion rules under §1738. The federal court must identify each binding common issue, with the required identity and full and fair opportunity to litigate; genuinely distinct issues not necessarily adjudicated remain outside that issue-preclusion bar.

The issue-specific rule in Dodd governs even when the claimant pursued state compensation procedures to satisfy a prerequisite to federal litigation. Federal courts give the state judgment its lawful preclusive effect issue by issue, preserving the distinction between a reserved cause and a binding determination. State litigation alone does not establish that every later federal issue is barred.

### 2005-06-20 — [Eulitt v. Maine Department of Education; 386 F.3d 344 (First Circuit).](../records/eulitt-v-maine-department-of-education_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

The opinion of the Court commands all nine votes on both holdings. Its author and complete joins are stated in Opinion Topology; each holding is directly supported at the stated level of generality.

#### 1. Equal treatment depends on the educational work publicly purchased

**Holding and operative rule:** Equal protection does not require a State to purchase integrated devotional instruction when its educational program purchases eligible secular work. Religious identity alone cannot disqualify an independently identifiable, reliably separated secular service that meets lawful neutral program requirements and effective use controls; the parents' request for ordinary St. Dominic's tuition to obtain integrated Catholic instruction falls on the devotional-purchase side of that distinction.

**Controlling explanation:** Agostini requires the Court to identify the government's actual function. Strout applies that inquiry to ordinary integrated tuition at St. Dominic's, while Zelman and McCallum establish that parental choice and payment routing do not change the function a restricted payment buys. The parents identify Catholic instruction and viewpoint as the need the public school does not meet. Their requested tuition purchases that integrated instructional package.

The equality objection is substantial when religious affiliation excludes otherwise eligible secular work. Columbia Union and K.D.M. protect participation on equal terms in such work, and the present rule preserves that protection. Here the financed services differ in the constitutionally material respect established by the governing cases. The program's support for private secular alternatives therefore does not require the requested devotional purchase. The educational-needs condition, school-approval requirements and sectarian exclusion remain distinct; the Court decides the actual funding claim rather than presuming an additional denial under an unapplied neutral criterion.

Strout resolved its granted Religion Clause questions. Its rule supplies substantive authority here; it did not previously adjudicate these different parents' equality claim. Locke's permission concerning an individual ministry-degree scholarship likewise cannot become a general license to exclude religious institutions. These distinctions answer the parents' equal-access argument while retaining equal eligibility for genuinely controlled secular services.

#### 2. A close relationship does not replace a genuine obstacle to the school's own suit

**Holding and operative rule:** Parents seeking to assert a school's separate constitutional rights must establish a genuine hindrance to the school's own assertion, in addition to their own injury and a sufficiently close relationship. These parents have an actual relationship with St. Dominic's, but have not established the independent obstacle required to litigate its institutional claim.

**Controlling explanation:** Kowalski requires examination of the relationship and obstacle as separate prerequisites. Its hypothetical future clients differ from the identified school here. That difference satisfies neither the missing obstacle nor a general entitlement to litigate another legal person's claims. The parents' purchase of the school's services and the financial consequences of the exclusion explain their personal controversy, which the Court has decided. They do not show why the school cannot present its own rights. The institutional claim is therefore rejected at the representative-standing threshold.

- Agostini v. Felton supplies the unified governmental-act-and-function inquiry; the decision applies that inquiry to the public purchase actually requested.
- Strout v. Albanese supplies the integrated-tuition rule, applied to a distinct equal-protection claim rather than treated as an earlier adjudication of that claim.
- Zelman v. Simmons-Harris and Freedom From Religion Foundation v. McCallum establish that neutral selection and private routing do not alter the devotional function bought by a restricted public payment; both retain their stated boundaries.
- Columbia Union College v. Oliver and K.D.M. v. Reorganized School District No. 7 preserve equal eligibility for genuine secular services under their separation, accountability and service-specific requirements.
- Locke v. Davey retains its bounded ministry-training holding in the individual-scholarship setting.
- Kowalski v. Tesmer is applied to the independent requirements for third-party assertion, with its absent-client circumstance distinguished from the existing relationship here.

The existing financed-function framework now supplies the direct answer to the equality theory presented by an ordinary integrated devotional-tuition request. Eligible controlled secular work remains open to religious providers on equal terms. The third-party-standing rule continues to require a supported institutional hindrance even when the claimant has a close existing relationship with the rights holder.

### 2005-06-20 — [Mid-Con Freight Systems, Inc. v. Michigan Public Service Commission, No. 03-1234.](../records/mid-con-freight-systems-v-michigan-public-service-commission_merits_2005-06-20.md#public-projection)

**Holding and operative rule:** Section 14504 preempts additional state registration obligations concerning the federal system's specified subjects—evidence of federal operating authority, insurance, a service agent and associated registration fees—when those obligations exceed the federal standards. It does not displace a genuinely separate state regulatory obligation on another subject merely because it applies to interstate carriers; Michigan's section 478.2(2) charge falls outside the covered subjects on this record.

**Authority:** The signed opinion of the Court, with the seven complete joins stated in Opinion Topology, directly controls the rule, application and remedy.

**Controlling explanation:** Both sentences of section 14504(b) measure registration against the Secretary's standards. Subsection (a) defines those standards by their forms-and-procedures function, and subsection (c) identifies the federal-registration system. The repeated registration language concerns that defined subject. The excess-obligation prohibition therefore protects the federal system against additional covered demands without absorbing every state carrier-registration program.

The competing interstate-status reading would treat every registration requirement triggered by interstate operation as covered, while preserving general neutral registration. That distinction is narrower than a universal fee cap, but the statute identifies the transportation governed by the federal system; it does not say that an obligation on a different subject becomes covered whenever interstate operation triggers it. Dividing the work between subsections (b) and (c) cannot overcome their common reference to the defined system. The replacement system's centralization of relevant filings and fees confirms the enacted structure. Yellow Transportation enforces the benchmark within that system and preserves unrelated taxes and licensing policies.

Michigan's separate program seeks vehicle information and a fee rather than the listed federal permit, insurance or service-agent filings. The apparent $10 waiver creates an administrative connection but does not establish that payment of the $100 is necessary to complete federal registration. A fee's label, age or regulatory purpose alone would not establish independence; the actual obligation supplies the distinction here. Because general neutrality is not the adopted test, the dispute over the combined coverage of Michigan's two provisions does not require a characterization remand under this rule.

**Operative qualifications:** Covered federal-registration demands remain subject to the federal standards despite a different state label. The insurance-proof fee system retains its November 15, 1991 benchmark and $10 ceiling. The prohibition on charging for filing and maintaining evidence of federal registration is distinct. The vehicle-identification restriction governs what the Secretary's standards may require, and the separate inconsistent-fee provision concerns fees under the federal system. These commands do not create a general $10 ceiling for state carrier regulation.

Yellow Transportation, Inc. v. Michigan is applied for its enforcement of the delegated registration benchmark, qualifying reciprocity and express system-specific boundary. The present decision resolves which subject section 14504 governs; Yellow's separate fee-calculation holding remains in force.

The federal registration ceiling governs demands within the Single State Registration System's specified subjects. Courts must identify the actual information, payment and conditions a state program imposes. A separate program on a different subject remains outside that ceiling, while relabeling an additional covered demand cannot evade it. Michigan's challenged fee satisfies that distinction on the present record.

### 2005-06-23 — [Exxon Mobil Corp. v. Allapattah Services, Inc., No. 04-70; del Rosario Ortega v. Star-Kist Foods, Inc., No. 04-79.](../records/exxon-mobil-v-allapattah-services_merits_2005-06-23.md#public-projection)

**Holding and operative rule:** When at least one named plaintiff satisfies the diversity amount requirement and the action otherwise meets original-jurisdiction requirements, section 1367(a) authorizes additional claims forming the same Article III case, including claims by additional parties, unless an enacted exception excludes them. Rule 23 class membership and the Rule 20 plaintiff joinder presented here create no such exclusion; the related smaller claims may proceed subject to the statute and applicable procedural rules.

**Authority:** The five Justices joining the complete Opinion of the Court control both docket components and every proposition below.

**Controlling explanation:** The qualifying named claim supplies an action within original jurisdiction. The amount of another plaintiff's claim does not eliminate that independently sufficient claim. Subsection (a) then expressly reaches related claims involving additional parties. Requiring independent jurisdiction over every such claim would defeat the grant's work. City of Chicago applies this distinction between original power, related-claim authority and discretion.

Subsection (b) identifies particular diversity exclusions. It distinguishes claims against persons brought into the case under designated rules from claims by particular added plaintiffs. Congress omitted Rule 23 members and Rule 20 plaintiffs from the latter categories. The Court cannot insert those exclusions to preserve every feature of the earlier amount regime. Finley required legislative authorization for additional-party jurisdiction; Congress supplied it. Zahn and Clark therefore yield for the claims this grant covers.

The competing original-action reading treats every amount deficiency as destructive of the entire action. That approach fails to give the additional-claims language its enacted effect. Complete diversity remains independently necessary: Grupo Dataflux preserves the citizenship requirement, while Caterpillar addresses an actual cure through dismissal of a party. Neither makes a smaller related claim destroy an otherwise sufficient diversity action. Asserted legislative expectations cannot add exclusions absent from the enacted structure.

The dealer and family claims present the same statutory problem. Each must have the required relationship to the qualifying claim and satisfy the applicable procedural rules. Section 1367(c) permits lawful declination after authority is established; it does not make retention automatic.

**Operative qualifications:** The rule preserves complete diversity and requires an independently qualifying original claim; separate claims cannot be aggregated to manufacture that claim. In diversity-only actions, subsection (b) excludes claims by plaintiffs against persons made parties under Rules 14, 19, 20 or 24, and claims by persons proposed as plaintiffs under Rule 19 or seeking to intervene as plaintiffs under Rule 24, when exercising jurisdiction would be inconsistent with section 1332.
